`Mattā` is used for measure, lordship, syllabic length, and a small amount. `Sutta`, in all three genders, is used for a discourse, for that which is void, for a thread or doctrine, and for a dream.

879.

879.

Rājaliṅgo’sabhaṅgesu, [M 0.0073] rukkhe ca kakudo kakudho (ṭī. sī.) pyatha;

Nimitta’kkhara sūpesu, byañjanaṃ cihane pade.

`Kakuda` is used for a royal emblem, a bull's hump, and a tree. `Byañjana` is used for a sign, a letter, curry, a mark, and a word.

880.

880.

Vohāre jetu micchāyaṃ, kīḷādo cāpi devanaṃ;

Bhariyāyaṃ tu kedāre, sarīre khetta mīritaṃ.

`Devana` is used for conventional speech, gambling, and play and so forth. But `khetta` is said of a wife, a field, and the body.

881.

881.

Sussūsāyañca viññeyyaṃ, issābhyāse pyu’pāsanaṃ;

Sūlaṃ tu nitthiyaṃ heti, bhede saṃku rujāsu ca.

`Upāsana` should be understood as obedience, archery, and practice. But `sūla`, in the neuter gender, is used for a weapon, for splitting, for a stake, and for pains.

882.

882.

Tanti vīṇāguṇe, tantaṃ, mukhyasiddhanta tantusu;

Rathādyaṅge tu ca yugo, kappamhi yugale yugaṃ.

`Tanti` is used for a lute-string; `tanta` for a primary doctrine and for a thread. `Yugo` is used for a chariot-yoke and so forth; `yugaṃ` for an aeon and a pair.

883.

883.

Itthipupphe ca reṇumhi, rajo pakatije guṇe;

Nyāsappaṇe tu dānamhi, niyyātana mudīritaṃ.

'Raja' is used for menstruation, for pollen, and for a natural quality; but 'niyyātana' is declared for depositing or entrusting, and for a gift.

884.

884.

Garu’pāyā’vatāresu, titthaṃ pūtambu diṭṭhisu;

Paṇḍake joti nakkhatta, raṃsīsva’ggimhi joti so.

'Tittha' is used for a venerable teacher, for a means, for a landing place, for pure water, and for views; 'joti' is used for a eunuch, for a star, for rays, and for fire.

885.

885.

Kaṇḍo nitthī sare daṇḍe, vagge cāvasare pyatha;

Uddhaṃbāhudvayamāne bāhudvayummāne (sī. ka.), sūrattepi ca porisaṃ.

'Kaṇḍa', in the masculine and neuter, is used for an arrow, a staff, a group, and also for an opportunity; 'porisa' is used for the measure of a man's height with arms upraised, and also for courage.

886.

886.

Uṭṭhānaṃ porise’hāsu, nisinnādyu’ggame pyatha;

Anissayamahībhāge, tvi’rīṇaṃ ūsare siyā.

'Uṭṭhāna' is used for human effort, for exertion, and also for rising from a seated position and so forth; 'irīṇa' may be used for a tract of land without support, and for barren land.

887.

887.

Ārādhanaṃ sādhane ca, pattiyaṃ paritosane;

Padhāne tu ca sānumhi, visāṇe siṅga muccate.

'Ārādhana' is used for accomplishing, for attainment, and for pleasing; 'siṅga' is said for that which is chief, for a mountain peak, and for a horn.

888.

888.

Diṭṭhā’dimagge ñāṇa’kkhi, kkhaṇa laddhīsu dassanaṃ;

Heme pañcasuvaṇṇe ca, nikkho nitthī pasādhane.

'Dassana' is used for seeing, for the path, for the wisdom-eye, for a moment, and for doctrines; 'nikkha', in the masculine and neuter, is used for gold, for a weight of five suvaṇṇas, and for an adornment.

889.

889.

Tithibhede ca sākhādi, phaḷumhi pabba muccate;

Nāgaloke tu pātālaṃ, bhāsitaṃ balavāmukhe.

'Pabba' is said for a division of a lunar day, for a branch and so forth, and for a joint; but 'pātāla' is spoken of for the Nāga world, and for the submarine fire.

890.

890.

Kāmaje kopaje dose, byasanañca vipattiyaṃ;

Atho’pakaraṇe siddhi, kārakesu ca sādhanaṃ.

'Byasana' is used for faults born of desire and anger, and for misfortune; and 'sādhana' is used for an instrument, for success, and for causal agents.

891.

891.

Tīsvīrito tīsvito (ṭī.) dānasīle, vadaññū vagguvādini;

Purakkhato bhisitte ca, pūjite puratokate.

One who is generous, virtuous, bountiful, and sweet-spoken is called 'vadaññū'; one who is honored, anointed, worshipped, and placed in front is called 'purakkhato'. These terms are used in the three genders.

892.

892.

Mando bhāgyavihīne cā, ppake mūḷhā’paṭūsvapi;

Vuddhiyutte samunnaddhe, uppanne ussitaṃ bhave.

'Mando' is used for one who is deprived of fortune, for the weak, for the confused, and for the unskilled; 'ussitaṃ' may be used for that which is developed, elevated, or arisen.

893.

893.

Rathaṅge’kkho [M 0.0074] suvaṇṇasmiṃ, pāsake, akkha mindriye;

Sassate ca dhuvo tīsu, dhuvaṃ takke ca nicchite.

'Akkha' is used for the axle of a chariot, for a golden die, for a die, and for a sense faculty; 'dhuva' is an adjective in three genders meaning eternal or stable; as an adverb, 'dhuvaṃ' is used for that which is certain or determined.

894.

894.

Hare sivo, sivaṃ bhadda, mokkhesu, jambuke sivā;

Senāyaṃ sattiyañceva, thūlatte ca balaṃ bhave.

'Sivo' (masculine) is used for Hara; 'sivaṃ' (neuter) is used for that which is auspicious and for liberation; 'sivā' (feminine) is used for a jackal. 'Balaṃ' may be used for an army, for a spear, and for stoutness.

895.

895.

Saṅkhyā narakabhedesu, padumaṃ vārije pyatha;

Devabhede vasu pume, paṇḍakaṃ ratane dhane.

'Saṅkhyā' is used for a number, and for a division of hell; 'paduma' is used for a lotus, which is born from water. 'Vasu', in the masculine, is used for a class of devas; 'paṇḍaka' is used for a gem and for wealth.

896.

896.

Nibbānaṃ atthagamane, apavagge siyā tha ca;

Setambuje puṇḍarīkaṃ, byagghe rukkhantare pume.

'Nibbāna' may be used for cessation and for liberation; 'puṇḍarīka' is used for a white lotus; 'byaggha', in the masculine, is used for a tiger and for a particular kind of tree.

897.

897.

Upahāre bali pume, karasmiṃcā’surantare;

Sukkaṃ tu sambhave, sukko, dhavale, kusale tisu.

'Bali', in the masculine, is used for an offering, for a tax, and for a particular asura; 'sukkaṃ' (neuter) is used for semen; the adjective 'sukka', in the three genders, is used for that which is white and for that which is skillful.

898.

898.

Dāyo dāne vibhattabba, dhane ca pitunaṃ vane dhane (ṭī.);

Pabhuttā’yattatā’yattā’, bhilāsesu vaso bhave.

'Dāyo' is used for a gift, for wealth to be distributed, and for an ancestral forest; 'vaso' may be used for mastery, for dependence, for that which is dependent, and for desires.

899.

899.

Paribhāsana makkose, niyame bhāsane tha ca,

Dhanamhi seḷanaṃ yodha, sīhanādamhi dissati.

'Paribhāsana' is used for abuse, and also for regulated speech or definition; 'seḷanaṃ' is seen to be used for wealth, for a warrior, and for a lion's roar.

900.

900.

Pabhavo jātihetumhi, ṭhāne cādyupaladdhiyaṃ;

Atho’tu nāripupphasmiṃ, hemantādimhi ca dvisu.

'Pabhavo' is used for the cause of birth, for a place, and for primary attainment; 'utu', in two senses, is used for menstruation, and for a season such as winter.

901.

901.

Karaṇaṃ sādhakatame, kriyā gattesu indriye;

Tāto tāḷo (sī.) tu kuñcikāyañca, tūriyaṅge dumantare.

'Karaṇaṃ' is used for the most effective means, for action, for the limbs, and for a sense faculty; 'tāto' is used for a key, for a part of a musical instrument, and for a hollow in a tree.

902.

902.

Pupphe phale ca pasavo, uppāde gabbhamocane;

Gāyane gāyake asse, gandhabbo devatāntare.

'Pasavo' is used for a flower and a fruit, for production, and for childbirth; 'gandhabbo' is used for singing, for a singer, for a horse, and for a particular kind of deity.

903.

903.

Vinā pupphaṃ phalaggāhi, rukkhe vanappati;

Āhate hemarajate, rūpiyaṃ rajatepi ca.

'Vanappati' is a tree that bears fruit without flowering, or a large forest tree in general; 'rūpiyaṃ' is used for wrought gold and silver, and also specifically for silver.

904.

904.

Khagādibandhane pāso, kesapubbo caye pyatha;

Tārā’kkhimajjhe nakkhatte, tāro uccatarassare.

'Pāso' is used for a snare for birds and other creatures, for a lock of hair, and also for a heap; 'tārā' is used for the pupil of the eye and for a star; 'tāro' is used for a high-pitched sound.

905.

905.

Patte ca lohabhedasmiṃ, kaṃso catukahāpaṇe;

Majjhimo dehamajjhasmiṃ, majjhabhave ca so tisu.

'Kaṃso' is used for a bowl, for a type of metal such as bronze, and for a measure of four kahāpaṇas; 'majjhimo', in the three genders, is used for the middle of the body and for an intermediate state of existence.

906.

906.

Āvesanaṃbhūtāvese, sippasālā gharesu ca;

Sobhā sampattīsu sirī, lakkhītthī devatāya ca.

'Āvesanaṃ' is used for spirit possession, for a workshop, and for a house; 'sirī' is used for splendor, for prosperity, for fortune, and for the goddess Lakkhī.

907.

907.

Kumāro [M 0.0075] yuvarāje ca, khande vutto susumhi ca;

Athā’nitthī pavāḷo ca, maṇibhede tathā’ṅkure.

'Kumāro' is spoken of for an heir-apparent, for Skanda, and for a youth; 'pavāḷo', which is not feminine, is used for a type of gem such as coral, and likewise for a sprout.

908.

908.

Paṇo vetana mūlesu, vohāre ca dhane mato;

Paṭiggaho tu gahaṇe, kathito bhājanantare.

'Paṇo' is considered to be a wage, capital, trade, and wealth; 'paṭiggaho' is spoken of for acceptance, and for a receptacle.

909.

909.

Asubhe ca subhe kamme, bhāgyaṃ vuttaṃ dvaye pyatha;

Pipphalaṃ tarubhede ca, vatthacchedanasatthake.

'Bhāgyaṃ' is said to arise from both impure and pure actions; 'pipphalaṃ' is used for a particular kind of tree, and for a tool used for cutting cloth.

910.

910.

Apavaggo pariccāgā’, vasānesu vimuttiyaṃ;

Liṅgaṃ tu aṅgajātasmiṃ, pumattādimhi lakkhaṇe.

'Apavaggo' is used for abandonment, for endings, and for liberation; 'liṅgaṃ' is used for the generative organ, for gender such as masculinity, and for a characteristic.

911.

911.

Cāge sabhāve nimmāne, saggo jjhāye divepyatha;

Rohito lohite maccha, bhede ceva migantare.

'Saggo' is used for generosity, for nature, for creation, for meditation, and for heaven; 'rohito' is used for the color red, for a type of fish, and for a type of deer.

912.

912.

Niṭṭhā nipphattiyaṃ cevā, vasānamhi adassane;

Kaṇṭako tu sapattasmiṃ, rukkhaṅge lomahaṃsane.

'Niṭṭhā' is used for accomplishment, for the end, and for disappearance; 'kaṇṭako' is used for an enemy, for a thorn on a tree, and for the bristling of hair.

913.

913.

Mukhyo’pāyesu vadane, ādismiṃ mukha mīritaṃ;

Dabbaṃ bhabbe guṇādhāre, vitte ca budha dārusu.

Mukha is said to mean chief among means, in the mouth, and in the beginning. Dabbaṃ is said to mean in the able, in a receptacle of virtues, in wealth, in the wise, and in wood.

914.

914.

Mānaṃ pamāṇe patthādo, māno vutto vidhāya ca;

Atho parissame vutto, vāyāmo vīriyepi ca.

Mānaṃ, a neuter noun, means in measurement and in a measure. Māno, a masculine noun, is spoken of in pride. Vāyāmo means in exertion, and also in energy.

915.

915.

Saroruhe satapattaṃ, satapatto khagantare;

Chidde tu chiddavante ca, susiraṃ tūriyantare.

Satapattaṃ, a neuter noun, means in a lotus. Satapatto, a masculine noun, means among birds. Susiraṃ means in a hole, in the perforated, and in a musical instrument.

916.

916.

Ekasmiṃ sadise sante, samānaṃ vāccaliṅgikaṃ;

Atho gārava bhītīsu, saṃvege sambhamo mato.

Samānaṃ, which applies to all genders, means existing in one that is similar. Sambhamo is considered to mean in reverence, in fear, and in agitation.

917.

917.

Juṇhā candappabhāyañca, tadupetanisāya ca;

Vimānaṃ devatāvāse, sattabhūmigharamhi ca.

Juṇhā means in moonlight, and in the night accompanied by it. Vimānaṃ means in the abode of deities, and in a seven-storied dwelling.

918.

918.

Māse jeṭṭho, tivuddhā’ti, ppasatthesu ca tīsu so;

Dhamme ca maṅgale seyyo, so pasatthatare tisu.

Jeṭṭho means the month Jeṭṭha, an elder, and the most praised. Seyyo, in three genders, means in the Dhamma, in the auspicious, and in the better.

919.

919.

Ādiccādimhi gahaṇe, nibandhe ca ghare gaho;

Kāco tu mattikābhede, sikkāyaṃ nayanāmaye.

Gaho means in the seizing of the sun and other celestial bodies, in confinement, and in a house. Kāco means in a kind of crystal, in a carrying-strap, and in an eye disease.

920.

920.

Tīsu gāmaṇi seṭṭhasmiṃ, adhipe gāmajeṭṭhake;

Bimbaṃ tu paṭibimbe ca, maṇḍale bimbikāphale.

Gāmaṇi has three meanings: the best, an overlord, and a village headman. Bimbaṃ means in an image, in a reflection, in a disc, and in the bimbikā fruit.

921.

921.

Bhājanādi parikkhāre, bhaṇḍaṃ mūladhanepi ca;

Maggo tvariyamagge ca, sammādiṭṭhādike, pathe.

Bhaṇḍaṃ means in vessels and other requisites, and also in capital funds. Maggo means in the Noble Path, in right view and the like, and in a path.

922.

922.

Samā [M 0.0076] vasse, samo kheda, santīsu, so nibhe tisu;

Cāpetvissāsa, musuno, issāso khepakamhi ca.

Samā means in a year. Samo means in fatigue and in peaceful states; and in the sense of 'similar,' it is in three genders. Issāso means in a bow and in an archer.

923.

923.

Bālo tīsvā’divayasā, samaṅgini apaṇḍite;

Rattaṃ tu soṇite, tambā, nuratta, rañjite tisu.

Bālo has three meanings: in early age, in one who is endowed, and in the unwise. Rattaṃ means in blood; and as an adjective in three genders, it means copper-colored, impassioned, and dyed.

924.

924.

Tace kāye ca tanvitthī, tīsva’ppe viraḷe kise;

Utubhede tu sisiro, hime so sītale tisu.

Tanu means in the skin and in the body; as a feminine noun, it means a slender woman. As an adjective in three genders, it means little, sparse, and emaciated. Sisiro means a type of season and frost; as an adjective in three genders, it means cold.

925.

925.

Sakkharā guḷabhede ca, kathalepi ca dissati;

Anuggahe tu saṅkhepe, gahaṇe saṅgaho mato.

Sakkharā is seen to mean a type of sugar and gravel. Saṅgaho is considered to mean support, summary, and grasping.

926.

926.

Dakkhe ca tikhiṇe byatte, rogamutte paṭuttisu;

Rājā tu khattiye vutto, naranāthe pabhumhi ca.

Paṭu, in three genders, means skillful, sharp, intelligent, and free from disease. Rājā is spoken of to mean a Khattiya, a lord of men, and a master.

927.

927.

Khalañca dhaññakaraṇe, kakke nīce khalo bhave;

Athu’ppāde samudayo, samūhe paccayepi ca.

Khalaṃ means a threshing floor; khalo means dregs and a wicked person. Samudayo means arising, a multitude, and also a condition.

928.

928.

Brahmacārī gahaṭṭhādo, assamo ca tapovane;

Bhayaṅkare tu kaṭhine, kurūro tīsu niddaye.

Assamo means the stages of life such as the celibate and the householder, and also an ascetic grove. Kurūro, in three genders, means fearful, harsh, and pitiless.

929.

929.

Kaniṭṭho kaniyo tīsu, atyappe’tiyuve pyatha;

Sīghamhi lahu taṃ, iṭṭha, nissārā’garusuttisu.

Kaniṭṭho and kaniyo, in three genders, mean very little and very young. Lahu means swift, desired, worthless, not heavy, and slothful.

930.

930.

Adharo tīsvadho hīne, pume dantacchade pyatha;

Sussusā sotu micchāya, sā pāricariyāya ca.

Adharo, in three genders, means lower and inferior; as a masculine noun, it also means in the lip. Sussusā means in the desire to hear and in attendance.

931.

931.

Hattho pāṇimhi ratane, gaṇe soṇḍāya bhantare;

Āvāṭe udapāne ca, kūpo kumbhe ca dissati.

Hattho means in the hand, in a cubit, in a multitude, and in an elephant's trunk. Kūpo is seen to mean in a pit, in a well, and in a mast.

932.

932.

Ādo padhāne paṭhamaṃ, pamukhañca tiliṅgikaṃ;

Vajjabhede ca vita taṃ, taṃ vitthāre tiliṅgikaṃ.

Ādi, in three genders, means chief, first, and foremost. Vitataṃ, in three genders, means in a type of musical instrument and in expansion.

933.

933.

Sāro bale thiraṃse ca, uttame so tiliṅgiko;

Bhāro tu khandhabhārādo, dvisahassapalepi ca.

Sāro, in three genders, means strength, a firm part, and the best. Bhāro means a shoulder burden and the like, and also two thousand palas.

934.

934.

Mandire rogabhede ca, khayo apacayamhi ca;

Vāḷo tu sāpade sappe, kurūre so tiliṅgiko.

Khayo means a dwelling, a type of disease, and decrease. Vāḷo means a beast of prey and a snake; as an adjective in three genders, it means cruel.

935.

935.

Sālo sajjaddume rukkhe, sālāgehe ca dissati;

Sote tu savanaṃ vuttaṃ, yajane sutiyampi ca.

Sālo is seen to mean the Sal tree, a tree, and a hall. Savanaṃ is said to mean the ear, and suti is said to mean sacrifice.

936.

936.

Tīsu pato pareto ca, mate ca petayonije;

Khyāte tu haṭṭhe viññāte, patītaṃ vāccaliṅgikaṃ.

Peto, in three genders, means departed, deceased, and born in the ghost-realm. Patītaṃ, which applies to all genders, means known, joyful, and understood.

937.

937.

Adhippāye [M 0.0077] ca ādhāre, āsayo kathito tha ca;

Pattaṃ pakkhe dale, patto, bhājane so gate tisu.

Āsayo is said to mean intention and receptacle. Pattaṃ means a wing and a leaf; patto means a bowl or vessel; as a past participle in three genders, it means reached.

938.

938.

Kusale sukataṃ, suṭṭhu, kate ca sukato tisu;

Tapassī tva’nukampāyā, rahe vutto tapodhane.

Sukataṃ means in the meritorious and in the well-done; sukato, in three genders, has the same meanings. Tapassī is said to mean in compassion, in seclusion, and in an ascetic.

939.

939.

Tīsu surādilolasmiṃ, soṇḍo hatthikare dvisu;

Assādane tu rasanaṃ, jivhāyañca dhanimhi ca.

Soṇḍo, in three genders, means addicted to intoxicants and the like; it also means an elephant's trunk. Rasanaṃ means savoring, the tongue, and sound.

940.

940.

Paṇīto tīsu madhure, uttame vihite pyatha;

Añjase visikhāyañca, pantiyaṃ vīthi nāriyaṃ.

Paṇīto, in three genders, means sweet, supreme, and also prepared. Vīthi, a feminine noun, means a path, a street, and a row.

941.

941.

Pāpasmiṃ gagane dukkhe, byasane cā’gha muccate;

Samūhe paṭalaṃ netta, roge vuttaṃ chadimhi ca.

Aghaṃ is said to mean evil, the sky, suffering, and disaster. Paṭalaṃ is said to mean a multitude, an eye disease, and a roof.

942.

942.

Sandhi saṅghaṭṭane vutto, sandhi’tthi paṭisandhiyaṃ;

Sattannaṃ pūraṇe seṭṭhe, tisante sattamo tisu.

Sandhi, as a masculine noun, is spoken of to mean connection; as a feminine noun, it means rebirth-linking. Sattamo means the seventh and the best; as an adjective in three genders, it also means peaceful.

943.

943.

Ojā tu yāpanāyañca, ojo ditti balesu ca;

Atho nisāmanaṃ vuttaṃ, dassane savanepi ca.

`Ojā` is used for sustenance, and `ojo` for splendor and strength. Furthermore, `nisāmanaṃ` is said to mean seeing and also hearing.

944.

944.

Gabbho kucchiṭṭhasatte ca, kucchi ovarakesu ca;

Khaṇḍane tva’padānañca, itivutte ca kammani.

`Gabbho` is used for a being in the womb, for the womb, and for inner chambers. `Apadāna` is used for breaking, for a biographical story, and for action.

945.

945.

Cittake rukkhabhede ca, tilako tilakāḷake;

Sīlādo paṭipatti’tthī, bodhe patti pavattisu.

`Tilako` is used for a sectarian mark, for a type of tree, and for a dark mole. `Patti`, a feminine noun, is used for the practice of virtue and the like, for enlightenment, and for occurrence.

946.

946.

Asumhi āyumhi (ṭī.), 407-gāthā passitabbā ca bale pāṇo, satte hadayagā’nile;

Chando vase adhippāye, vede’cchā’nuṭṭhubhādisu.

`Pāṇo` is used for breath, for strength, for a living being, and for the wind in the heart. `Chando` is used for will, for intention, for the Vedas, for desire, and for meters such as Anuṣṭubh.

947.

947.

Kāmoghādo, samūhasmiṃ, oghovege jalassa ca;

Kapālaṃ sirasaṭṭhimhi, ghaṭādi sakalepi ca.

`Ogho` is used for the flood of sensual desire and the like, for a multitude, and for the force of water. `Kapālaṃ` is used for the skull-bone, for a pot and the like, and also for a fragment.

948.

948.

Veṇvādisākhājālasmiṃ, laggakese jaṭā’laye;

Saraṇaṃ tu vadhe gehe, rakkhitasmiñca rakkhaṇe.

`Jaṭā` is used for a network of branches such as bamboo, for tangled hair, and for a matted dwelling. `Saraṇaṃ` is used for killing, for a house, for that which is protected, and for protection.

949.

949.

Thiyaṃ kantā piye, kanto, manuññe, so tiliṅgiko;

Gavakkhe tu samūhe ca, jālaṃ macchādibandhane.

The feminine `kantā` is used for a beloved. The adjective `kanta`, meaning pleasing, is of three genders. `Jālaṃ` is used for a window, for a multitude, and for a net for catching fish and the like.

950.

950.

Pucchāyaṃ garahāyañcā, niyame kiṃ tiliṅgikaṃ;

Sasaddhe tīsu nivāpe, saddhaṃ, saddhā ca paccaye.

`Kiṃ` is used for questioning, for censure, and for certainty; it is of three genders. The adjective `saddha`, meaning faithful, is of three genders. The neuter `saddhaṃ` is used for a funeral offering, and the feminine `saddhā` for faith.

951.

951.

Bījaṃ [M 0.0078] hetumhi aṭṭhismiṃ, aṅgajāte ca dissati;

Pubbo pūye’ggato aggate (ṭī.) ādo,

So disādo tiliṅgiko.

`Bījaṃ` is seen to mean cause, a seed-stone, and the generative organ. `Pubbo` means pus. The adjective `pubba`, meaning foremost or first, and in the sense of direction and the like, is of three genders.

952.

952.

Phalacitte hetukate, lābhe dhaññādike phalaṃ;

Āgamane tu dīghādi, nikāyesu ca āgamo.

`Phalaṃ` is used for fruition-consciousness, for the result of a cause, for gain, and for grain and the like. `Āgamo` is used for arrival and for the scriptural collections such as the Dīgha Nikāya.

953.

953.

Santāno devarukkhe ca, vutto santatiyaṃ pyatha;

Uttaraviparīte ca, seṭṭhe cā’nuttaraṃ tisu.

`Santāno` is said to mean a celestial tree and also continuity. `Anuttaraṃ`, being of three genders, means that which has no superior and that which is supreme.

954.

954.

Sattisampattiyaṃ vutto, kantimatte ca vikkamo;

Chāyā tu ātapābhāve, paṭibimbe pabhāya ca.

`Vikkamo` is said to mean the attainment of power and also a measure of beauty. `Chāyā` is used for shade, for a reflection, and for radiance.

955.

955.

Gimhe ghammo nidāgho ca, uṇhe sedajale pyatha;

Kappanaṃ kantane vuttaṃ, vikappe sajjane’tthiyaṃ.

`Ghammo` is used for summer, the hot season, for warmth, and also for sweat. `Kappanā`, a feminine noun, is said to mean cutting, an alternative, and preparation.

956.

956.

Aṅgā dese bahumha’ṅgaṃ, aṅgo dese vapumha’ṅga (ṭī.) tathā’vayavahetusu;

Devālaye ca thūpasmiṃ, cetiyaṃ cetiya’ddume.

The plural `Aṅgā` and masculine `aṅgo` are used for a country. `Aṅgo` is also used for the body, a constituent part, and a cause. `Cetiyaṃ` is used for a shrine, a stūpa, and a sacred tree.

957.

957.

Sajjano sādhupurise, sajjanaṃ kappane pyatha;

Supinaṃ supine sutta, viññāṇe ta manitthiyaṃ.

`Sajjano` is used for a virtuous person. `Sajjanaṃ` is used for preparation. `Supinaṃ`, a neuter noun, is used for a dream, for sleep, and for dream-consciousness.

958.

958.

Paccakkhe sannidhāne ca, sannidhi parikittito;

Bhiyyo bahutaratthe so, punaratthe’byayaṃ bhave.

`Sannidhi` is declared to mean presence and proximity. `Bhiyyo` is used in the sense of 'more' or 'much'; in the sense of 'again', it is an indeclinable.

959.

959.

Visalittasare diddho, diddho litte tiliṅgiko;

Vāse dhūmādisaṅkhāre, dhivāso sampaṭicchane.

The noun `diddho` means a poison-smeared arrow. The adjective `diddha`, meaning smeared, is of three genders. `Adhivāso` is used for enduring, for perfuming with incense, and for acceptance.

960.

960.

Vutto visārado tīsu, suppagabbhe ca paṇḍite;

Atha sitthaṃ madhucchiṭṭhe, vuttaṃ odanasambhave.

The adjective `visārada` is said to be of three genders, meaning eloquent and wise. Furthermore, `sitthaṃ` is said to mean beeswax and a grain of boiled rice.

961.

961.

Drave vaṇṇe rasabhede, kasāyo surabhimhi ca;

Atho uggamanaṃ vuttaṃ, uppattu’ddhagatīsu ca.

`Kasāyo` is used for an astringent decoction, for a reddish-brown color, for a type of taste, and for fragrance. Furthermore, `uggamanaṃ` is said to mean arising and upward movement.

962.

962.

Lūkhe niṭṭhuravācāyaṃ, pharusaṃ vāccaliṅgikaṃ;

Pavāho tva’mbuvege ca, sandissati pavattiyaṃ.

The adjective `pharusa`, being of three genders, means harsh or rough in speech. `Pavāho` is seen to mean a current of water and continuity.

963.

963.

Nissaye tappare iṭṭhe, parāyaṇapadaṃ tisu;

Kavace vāravāṇe ca, nimmokepi ca kañcuko.

The word `parāyaṇa`, being of three genders, means support, devoted, and desired. `Kañcuko` is used for a coat of mail, for a screen, and also for a snake's slough.

964.

964.

Lohabhede [M 0.0079] mataṃ tambaṃ, tambo ratte tiliṅgiko;

Tīsu tva’vasitaṃ ñāte, avasānagate mataṃ.

The neuter `tambaṃ` is known as a type of metal, copper; the adjective `tamba`, meaning red, is of three genders. The adjective `avasita`, being of three genders, is known to mean understood and also finished.

965.

965.

Bodhane ca padāne ca, viññeyyaṃ paṭipādanaṃ;

Sele nijjaladese ca, devatāsu marū’rito.

`Paṭipādanaṃ` should be understood to mean explaining and bestowing. `Maru` is said to mean a mountain, a waterless region, and deities.

966.

966.

Satthaṃ āyudha ganthesu, lohe, sattho ca sañcaye;

Jīvikāyaṃ vivaraṇe, vattane vutti nāriyaṃ.

The neuter `satthaṃ` is used for a weapon, a treatise, and metal. The masculine `sattho` is used for a caravan. `Vutti`, a feminine noun, is used for livelihood, for explanation, and for conduct.

967.

967.

Vīriye sūrabhāve ca, kathīyati parakkamo;

Atha kambu mato saṅkhe, suvaṇṇe valayepi ca.

`Parakkamo` is said to mean energy and heroism. Furthermore, `kambu` is known to mean a conch, gold, and also a bracelet.

968.

968.

Saro kaṇḍe akārādo, sadde vāpimhi’nitthiyaṃ;

Dupphasse tikhiṇe tīsu, gadrabhe kakace kharo.

`Saro`, a masculine noun, is used for an arrow, for a vowel such as 'a', for sound, and for a lake. The adjective `khara`, being of three genders, means rough to the touch and harsh. The noun `kharo` means a donkey and a saw.

969.

969.

Surāyu’paddave kāmā, savādimhi ca āsavo;

Dehe vutto rathaṅge ca, caturo’padhisū’padhi.

`Āsavo` is used for liquor, for affliction, for sensual desires, and for that which flows. `Upadhi` is said to mean the body, a chariot part, and the four substrates of existence.

970.

970.

Vatthu’ttaṃ kāraṇe dabbe, bhūbhede ratanattaye;

Yakkho deve mahārāje, kuverā’nucare nare.

`Vatthu` is said to mean a reason, a substance, a plot of land, and the Triple Gem. `Yakkho` is used for a deity, for a Great King, and for a man who is an attendant of Kuvera.

971.

971.

Dārukkhandhe pīṭhikāyaṃ, āpaṇe pīṭha māsane;

Parivāre parikkhāro, sambhāre ca vibhūsane.

`Pīṭhaṃ` is used for a log, a bench, a shop, and a stool. `Parikkhāro` is used for requisites, for equipment, and for adornment.

972.

972.

Vohārasmiñca ṭhapane, paññatti’tthī pakāsane;

Paṭibhānaṃ tu paññāyaṃ, upaṭṭhita girāya ca.

`Paññatti`, a feminine noun, is used for designation, for establishment, and for proclamation. `Paṭibhānaṃ` is used for intelligence and for ready speech.

973.

973.

Vacanāvayave mūle, kathito hetu kāraṇe;

Udare tu tathā pācā, nalasmiṃ gahaṇī’tthiyaṃ.

'Hetu' is said to mean a component of speech, a root, and a cause. 'Pācā' is used for the stomach, and 'gahaṇī' for a reed; the latter is feminine.

974.

974.

Piyo bhattari, jāyāyaṃ, piyā, iṭṭhe piyo tisu;

Yamarāje tu yugaḷe, saṃyame ca yamo bhave.

'Piyo' is used for a husband, and 'piyā' for a wife; the word 'piya' means 'desirable' and is of three genders. 'Yama' is used for the King Yama, a pair, and restraint.

975.

975.

Muddikassa ca pupphassa, rase khudde madhū’ritaṃ;

Ulloce tu ca vitthāre, vitānaṃ punnapuṃsake.

'Madhu' is said to mean grape, flower, juice, and honey. 'Vitāna' is used for a canopy and for expansion, and it is neuter.

976.

976.

Apavagge ca salile, sudhāyaṃ amataṃ mataṃ;

Mohe tu timire saṅkhyā, guṇe tama manitthiyaṃ.

'Amata' is known to mean liberation, water, and ambrosia. 'Tama' is used for delusion, darkness, a number, and a quality, and it is not feminine.

977.

977.

Khare cā’kāriye tīsu, rasamhi purise kaṭu;

Paṇḍake sukate, puññaṃ, manuññe pavane tisu.

'Kaṭu' means harsh and unsuitable, and is of three genders; in the sense of taste, it is masculine. 'Puñña' is used for a eunuch, a good deed, the delightful, and the pure, and is of three genders.

978.

978.

Rukkho dumamhi, pharusā, siniddhesu ca so tisu;

Uppattiyaṃ tu hetumhi, saṅge sukke ca sambhavo.

'Rukkha' means a tree; 'pharusa' is used for both rough and smooth and is of three genders. 'Sambhava' is used for origin, cause, attachment, and semen.

979.

979.

Nimittaṃ [M 0.0080] kāraṇe vuttaṃ, aṅgajāte ca lañchane;

Ādi sīmāpakāresu, samīpe’vayave mato.

'Nimitta' is said to mean a cause, a genital organ, and a sign. 'Ādi' is known to mean beginning, boundary, kind, proximity, and a part.

980.

980.

Vede ca mantane manto, mantā paññāya muccate;

Anayo byasane ceva, sandissati vipattiyaṃ.

'Manta' is used for the Veda and for counsel; 'mantā' is said to mean wisdom. 'Anaya' is seen to mean misfortune and ruin.

981.

981.

Aruṇo raṃsibhede cā, byattarāge ca lohite;

Anubandho tu pakatā, nivatte nassanakkhare.

'Aruṇa' is used for dawn, a kind of ray, a clear red hue, and blood. 'Anubandha' is used for continuity, nature, cessation, and a destroyed condition.

982.

982.

Avatāro’vataraṇe, titthamhi vivare pyatha;

Ākāro kāraṇe vutto, saṇṭhāne iṅgitepi ca.

'Avatāra' is used for descent, a landing place, and an opening. 'Ākāra' is said to mean a reason, a shape, and a gesture.

983.

983.

Sudditthi tanaye khattā, uggo, tibbamhi so tisu;

Padhānaṃ tu mahāmatte, pakatya’gga’dhitīsu ca.

'Khattā' is used for a pure woman and a son; 'ugga' means intense and is of three genders. 'Padhāna' is used for a chief minister, nature, excellence, and intellect.

984.

984.

Kallaṃ pabhāte, niroga, sajjadakkhesu yuttadakkhesu (ka.) tīsu taṃ;

Kuhanā kūṭacariyāyaṃ, kuhano kuhake tisu.

'Kalla' is used for dawn; as an adjective meaning healthy, ready, or skilled, it is of three genders. 'Kuhanā' is used for fraudulent behavior; 'kuhana' means a deceiver and is of three genders.

985.

985.

Kapoto pakkhibhede ca, diṭṭho pārāvate tha ca;

Sārado sāradabbhūte, apagabbhe mato tisu.

'Kapota' is seen to mean a kind of bird and also a pigeon. 'Sārada' is known to mean autumnal or born in autumn, and modest; in these senses, it is of three genders.

986.

986.

Tīsu khare ca kaṭhine, kakkaso sāhasappiye sāhasappiye=sāhasa+appiye (ṭī.);

Akāriye tu guyhaṅge, cīre kopīna muccate.

'Kakkasa' means harsh, hard, and fond of violence, and is of three genders. 'Kopīna' is said to mean something unsuitable, a private part, and a loincloth.

987.

987.

Migabhede paṭākāyaṃ, moce ca kadalī’tthiyaṃ;

Dakkhiṇā dānabhedasmiṃ, vāmato’ññamhi dakkhiṇo.

'Kadalī' is used for a kind of deer, a banner, and the banana tree, and is feminine. 'Dakkhiṇā' is used for a type of gift; 'dakkhiṇa' means right, as distinct from left.

988.

988.

Dutiyā bhariyāyañca, dvinnaṃ pūraṇiyaṃ matā;

Athuppāde siyā dhūma, ketu vessānarepi ca.

'Dutiyā' is known to mean a wife and the ordinal number second. 'Dhūmaketu' is used for a portent and also for fire.

989.

989.

Bhavaniggamane yāne, dvāre nissaraṇaṃ siyā;

Niyāmako potavāhe, tiliṅgo so niyantari.

'Nissaraṇa' is used for departure from existence, a vehicle, and a door. 'Niyāmaka' is used for a ship's pilot and a controller, and is of three genders.

990.

990.

Apavagge vināse ca, nirodho rodhane pyatha;

Bhaye paṭibhayaṃ vuttaṃ, tiliṅgaṃ taṃ bhayaṃkare.

'Nirodha' is used for cessation, destruction, and confinement. 'Paṭibhaya' is said to mean fear; as an adjective meaning terrible, it is of three genders.

991.

991.

Piṭakaṃ bhājane vuttaṃ, tatheva pariyattiyaṃ;

Jarāsithilacammasmiṃ, udaraṅge matā vali.

'Piṭaka' is said to mean a basket and likewise the scriptures. 'Vali' is known to mean a wrinkle of skin from old age and a fold of skin on the abdomen.

992.

992.

Bhinnaṃ vidārite’ññasmiṃ, nissite vāccaliṅgikaṃ;

Upajāpe mato bhedo, visese ca vidāraṇe.

'Bhinna' means split, different, or dependent, and is an adjective of three genders. 'Bheda' is known to mean dissension, distinction, and splitting.

993.

993.

Maṇḍalaṃ [M 0.0081] gāmasandohe, bimbe paridhirāsisu;

Āṇāya māgame lekhe; Sāsanaṃ anusāsane.

'Maṇḍala' is used for a cluster of villages, a disk, a circumference, and a heap. 'Sāsana' is used for a command, a scripture, a letter, and an instruction.

994.

994.

Agge tu sikharaṃ cā’yo, mayavijjhanakaṇṭake;

Guṇukkaṃse ca vibhave, sampatti ceva sampadā.

'Sikhara' is used for a peak and for an iron-tipped piercing thorn. 'Sampatti' and 'sampadā' are used for excellence of qualities and for prosperity.

995.

995.

Bhūkhantīsu khamā, yogye, hite sakke yutte (ṭī.) 1001-gāthā passitabbā khamo tisu;

Addho bhāge pathe kāle, ekaṃse’ddho’byayaṃ bhave;

Atho karīsaṃ vaccasmiṃ, vuccate caturambaṇe.

'Khamā' is used for the earth and for patience; 'khamo' means suitable, beneficial, or able, and is of three genders. 'Addha' is used for a half, a path, and time; as an indeclinable, 'addhā' means certainly. Then, 'karīsa' is said to mean excrement and a measure of four ambaṇas.

996.

996.

Usabho’sadha go usabho usabhe (ṭī.) seṭṭhe, sū’sabhaṃ vīsayaṭṭhiyaṃ;

Setusmiṃ tanti pantīsu, nāriyaṃ pāḷi kathyate.

'Usabha' is used for medicine, a bull, and the best. 'Usabha' is also used for a measure of twenty yaṭṭhis. 'Pāḷi' is said to mean a causeway, a cord, and a row, and is feminine.

997.

997.

Kaṭo jaye’tthinimitte, kilañje so kate tisu;

Mahiyaṃ jagatī vuttā, mandirālindavatthumhi.

'Kaṭa' is used for victory, a sign for a woman, and a mat; the adjective 'kata', meaning 'done', is of three genders. 'Jagatī' is said to mean the earth, a mansion, a veranda, and a foundation.

998.

998.

Vitakke mathite takko, tathā sūciphale mato;

Sudassanaṃ sakkapure, tīsu taṃ duddase’tare.

'Takka' is known to mean thought, buttermilk, and a needle-fruit. 'Sudassana' is used for Sakka's city; it is also used for 'easy to see', in which sense it is of three genders.

999.

999.

Dīpo’ntarīpa pajjota, patiṭṭhā nibbutīsu ca;

Baddhanissita setesu, tīsu taṃ mihite sitaṃ.

'Dīpa' is used for an island, a lamp, a support, and Nibbāna. 'Sita' is used for bound, dependent, white, and smiled, and is of three genders.

1000.

1000.

Thiyaṃ pajāpati dāre, brahme māre sure pume;

Vāsudeve’ntake kaṇho, so pāpe asite tisu.

'Pajāpati' is used for a wife in the feminine gender, and for Brahmā, Māra, and a deity in the masculine. 'Kaṇha' is used for Vāsudeva and Death; it also means evil and black, in which senses it is of three genders.

1001.

1001.

Upacāro upaṭṭhāne, āsanne aññaropane;

Sakko inde janapade, sākiye, so khame tisu.

'Upacāra' is used for service, proximity, and transference of meaning. 'Sakka' is used for Indra, a country, and a Sākiyan; it also means able or patient, in which sense it is of three genders.

1002.

1002.

Vajjane parihāro ca, sakkāre ceva rakkhaṇe;

Sotāpannādike agge, ariyo tīsu, dvije pume.

'Parihāra' is used for avoidance, honor, and protection. 'Ariya' is used for a noble one, such as a stream-enterer and so forth, and for the foremost; in these senses, it is of three genders. It is also used for a twice-born, in which sense it is masculine.

1003.

1003.

Susuko susumāre ca, bālake ca ulūpini;

Indīvaraṃ mataṃ nīlu, ppale uddālapādape.

'Susuka' is used in the sense of 'dolphin', 'child', and 'owl'. 'Indīvara' is known in the sense of 'blue water lily' and 'uddālaka tree'.

1004.

1004.

Asano piyake kaṇḍe, bhakkhaṇe khipane’ sanaṃ;

Yuge’dhikāre vikāre (ṭī.) vīriye, padhāne cā’ntike dhuro.

'Asana' is used in the sense of the 'piyaka tree', 'arrow', 'eating', and 'throwing'. 'Dhura' is used in the sense of 'yoke', 'authority', 'energy', 'chief', and 'proximity'.

1005.

1005.

Kāḷe ca bhakkhite tīsu, lavitte asito pume;

Pavāraṇā paṭikkhepe, kathitā’jjhesanāya ca.

'Asita' is used in the sense of 'black', 'eaten', and 'reaped', and is an adjective in three genders; as a masculine noun, it means 'sickle'. 'Pavāraṇā' is declared in the sense of 'refusal' and 'invitation'.

1006.

1006.

Ummāre [M 0.0082] esikatthambhe, indakhīlo mato tha ca;

Potthakaṃ makacivatthe, ganthe lepyādi kammani.

'Indakhīla' is known in the sense of 'threshold' and 'a city-gate pillar'. 'Potthaka' is used in the sense of 'a book', 'a spider's web', and 'plastering work'.

1007.

1007.

Dhaññaṃ sālyādike vuttaṃ, dhañño puññavati ttisu;

Pāṇi hatthe ca satte bhū, saṇhakaraṇiyaṃ mato.

'Dhañña' (neuter) is said to mean 'grain', such as rice and so forth; as an adjective, 'dhañña' means 'fortunate' and is in three genders. 'Pāṇi' is known in the sense of 'hand', 'a living being', and 'smoothing'.

1008.

1008.

Tīsu pītaṃ halidyābhe, haṭṭhe ca pāyite siyā;

Byūho nibbiddharacchāyaṃ, balanyāse gaṇe mato.

'Pīta', an adjective in three genders, may be used in the sense of 'yellow', 'joyful', and 'drunk'. 'Byūha' is known in the sense of 'a street layout', 'a deployment of forces', and 'a group'.

1009.

1009.

Lohitādimhi lobhe ca, rāgo ca rañjane mato;

Padaro phalake bhaṅge, pavuddha dariyaṃ pica.

'Rāga' is known in the sense of 'color', such as red and so forth, 'lust' or 'greed', and 'the act of coloring'. 'Padara' is used in the sense of 'a plank', 'a splitting', and 'a chasm'.

1010.

1010.

Siṅghāṭakaṃ kaserussa, phale, maggasamāgame;

Bahulāyañca kheḷamhi, eḷā, dose’ḷa mīritaṃ.

'Siṅghāṭaka' is used in the sense of the 'water chestnut fruit' and a 'crossroads'. 'Eḷā' is used for 'abundance' and 'saliva', while 'eḷa' is said to mean 'fault'.

1011.

1011.

Ādhāro cā’dhikaraṇe, pattādhāre’ lavālake;

Kāro’ gabhede sakkāre, kārā tu bandhanālaye.

'Ādhāra' is used in the sense of 'support', the 'locative case', a 'bowl-stand', and a 'basin for water'. 'Kāra' is used in the sense of 'an act', a 'type of tax', and 'honor'; 'kārā', however, is used for a 'prison'.

1012.

1012.

Karakā meghapāsāṇe, karako kuṇḍikāya ca;

Pāpane ca padātismiṃ, gamane patti nāriyaṃ.

'Karakā' is used in the sense of 'hail'; 'karaka' is used for a 'water-pot'. 'Patti', a feminine noun, is used in the sense of 'attainment', 'a foot-soldier', and 'going'.

1013.

1013.

Chiddaṃ randhañca vivaraṃ, susire dūsanepi ca;

Muttā tu muttike, muttaṃ, passāve, muccite tisu.

'Chidda', 'randha', and 'vivara' are used in the sense of a 'hole' or 'hollow', and also a 'fault'. 'Muttā' is used for a 'pearl'; 'muttaṃ' (neuter) for 'urine'; and 'mutta', as an adjective in three genders, means 'released'.

1014.

1014.

Nisedhe vāraṇaṃ, hatthi, liṅga hatthīsu vāraṇo;

Dānaṃ cāge made suddhe, khaṇḍane lavane khaye.

'Vāraṇaṃ' (neuter) is used in the sense of 'prohibition'; 'vāraṇo' (masculine) is used for an 'elephant'. 'Dāna' is used in the sense of 'giving', 'intoxication', 'purification', 'breaking', 'cutting', and 'destruction'.

1015.

1015.

Manotose ca nibbāne, tthaṅgame nibbuti’tthiyaṃ;

Negamo nigamubbhūte, tathā’paṇopajīvini.

'Nibbuti', a feminine noun, is used in the sense of 'mental satisfaction', 'Nibbāna', and 'cessation'. 'Negama' is used for one 'originating from a market town' and also for a 'trader'.

1016.

1016.

Haritasmiñca paṇṇe ca, palāso kiṃ sukaddume;

Pakāso pākaṭetīsu, ālokasmiṃ pume mato.

'Palāsa' is used in the sense of 'green', a 'leaf', and the 'Kiṃsuka tree'. 'Pakāsa' is known as an adjective in three genders meaning 'manifest', and as a masculine noun meaning 'light'.

1017.

1017.

Pakkaṃ phalamhi, taṃ nāsu, mmukhe nāsamukhe (ka.) pariṇate tisu;

Piṇḍo ājīvane dehe, piṇḍane goḷake mato.

'Pakka' is used for a 'ripe fruit'; as an adjective in three genders, it means 'mature' or 'cooked'. 'Piṇḍa' is known in the sense of 'livelihood', 'the body', 'lumping together', and 'a ball'.

1018.

1018.

Vaṭṭo paribbaye kammā, dike, so vaṭṭule tisu;

Paccāhāre paṭihāro, dvāre ca dvārapālake.

'Vaṭṭa' is used in the sense of 'expenditure' and 'duty and so forth'; as an adjective in three genders, it means 'round'. 'Paṭihāra' is used in the sense of 'withdrawal', a 'door', and a 'doorkeeper'.

1019.

1019.

Nāriyaṃ bhīru kathitā, bhīruke so tiliṅgiko;

Vikaṭaṃ gūthamuttādo, vikaṭo vikate tisu.

'Bhīru' (a coward) is said to be a feminine noun; but as 'bhīruka' (cowardly), it is an adjective in three genders. 'Vikaṭaṃ' (neuter) is used for 'excrement and urine'; 'vikaṭa', as an adjective in three genders, means 'deformed'.

1020.

1020.

Vāmaṃ [M 0.0083] sabyamhi, taṃ cāru, viparītesu tīsva’tha;

Saṅkhyābhede sarabye ca, cihaṇe lakkha muccate.

'Vāma', an adjective in three genders, is used in the sense of 'left', 'lovely', and 'opposite'. 'Lakkha' is said to be used in the sense of 'the number one hundred thousand', a 'target', and a 'mark'.

1021.

1021.

Seṇī’tthī samasippīnaṃ, gaṇe cā’valiyaṃ pica;

Sudhāyaṃ dhūliyaṃ cuṇṇo, cuṇṇañca vāsacuṇṇake.

'Seṇī', a feminine noun, is used for a 'guild of artisans' and a 'row'. 'Cuṇṇa' is used for 'lime', 'dust', and 'fragrant powder'.

1022.

1022.

Jetabbe’tippasatthe’ti, vuddhe jeyyaṃ tisū’ritaṃ;

Takke tu mathitaṃ hotyā,

Lolite mathito tisu.

'Jeyya', an adjective in three genders, is said to mean 'to be conquered', 'highly praised', and 'elder'. 'Mathitaṃ' (neuter) is 'buttermilk'; 'mathita', as an adjective in three genders, means 'churned' or 'agitated'.

1023.

1023.

Abbhuto’cchariye tīsu, paṇe cevā’bbhuto pume;

Mecako pucchamūlamhi, kaṇhepi mecako tisu.

'Abbhuta' is an adjective in three genders meaning 'marvelous'; as a masculine noun, it also means a 'wager'. 'Mecaka' is used for the 'root of a tail'; as an adjective in three genders, it also means 'black'.

1024.

1024.

Vasavattī pume māre, vasavattāpake tisu;

Sambhave cā’suci pume, amejjhe tīsu dissati.

'Vasavattī' is a masculine noun meaning 'Māra'; in the sense of 'one who wields power', it is an adjective in three genders. 'Asuci' is a masculine noun meaning 'impurity'; as an adjective in three genders, it is seen to mean 'unclean'.

1025.

1025.

Accho ikke pume vutto, pasannamhi tiliṅgiko;

Baḷise selabhede ca, vaṅko, so kuṭile tisu.

'Accha' is said to be a masculine noun meaning a 'bear'; as an adjective meaning 'clear', it is in three genders. 'Vaṅka' is used for a 'fishhook' and a 'bend in a mountain'; as an adjective in three genders, it means 'crooked'.

1026.

1026.

Kuṇapamhi chavo ñeyyo,

Lāmake so tiliṅgiko;

Sabbasmiṃ sakalo tīsu, addhamhi purise siyā.

'Chava' should be known as a 'corpse'; as an adjective meaning 'low', it is in three genders. 'Sakala', meaning 'all' or 'whole', is in three genders. 'Addha', meaning 'a half', may be masculine.

1027.

1027.

Candaggāhādike cevu, ppādo uppattiyaṃ pica;

Padussane padoso ca, kathito saṃvarīmukhe.

'Uppāda' is used in the sense of a 'portent', such as an eclipse and the like, and also in the sense of 'origin'. 'Padosa' is said to mean 'corruption' and 'the evening'.

1028.

1028.

Rudhire lohitaṃ vuttaṃ, rattamhi lohito tisu;

Uttamaṅge pume muddhā, muddho mūḷhe tiliṅgiko.

'Lohitaṃ' (neuter) is said to mean 'blood'; 'lohita', as an adjective in three genders, means 'red'. 'Muddhā' (masculine) means 'head' or 'highest part'; 'muddha', as an adjective in three genders, means 'foolish'.

1029.

1029.

Raṭṭhamhi vijitaṃ vuttaṃ, jite ca vijito tisu;

Parittaṃ tu parittāṇe, paritto tīsu appake.

'Vijitaṃ' (neuter) is said to mean 'kingdom'; 'vijita', as an adjective in three genders, means 'conquered'. 'Parittaṃ' (neuter) is used for 'protection'; 'paritta', as an adjective in three genders, means 'small' or 'limited'.

1030.

1030.

Kumbhaṇḍo devabhede ca, dissati vallijātiyaṃ;

Catutthaṃse pade pādo, paccantaselaraṃsisu.

'Kumbhaṇḍa' is seen to mean a 'class of deities' and also a 'species of gourd-vine'. 'Pāda' is used for a 'quarter of a verse', the 'foot of a mountain', and a 'ray of light'.

1031.

1031.

Vaṅgo lohantare vaṅgā, dese pume bahumhi ca;

Kammārabhaṇḍabhede ca, khaṭake muṭṭhi ca dvisu.

'Vaṅga' is used for 'tin'; 'Vaṅgā' (masculine plural) refers to a 'country'. 'Muṭṭhi' is used in two senses: for a 'cup' and for a type of 'blacksmith's tool'.

1032.

1032.

Ambaṇaṃ doṇiyaṃ ce’kā, dasadoṇappamāṇake;

Adhiṭṭhitiya mādhāre, ṭhāne’dhiṭṭhāna muccate.

'Ambaṇa' is used for a 'trough', and also for a 'measure of eleven doṇas'. 'Adhiṭṭhāna' is said to mean 'foundation', 'support', and 'place'.

1033.

1033.

Pume mahesī sugate, deviyaṃ nāriyaṃ matā;

Upaddave upasaggo, dissati pādikepi ca.

'Mahesī' is masculine in the sense of 'Sugata'; it is considered feminine in the sense of 'queen' or 'woman'. The word 'upasagga' is seen in the sense of 'calamity' and also 'foot-soldier'.

1034.

1034.

Vakkaṃ [M 0.0084] koṭṭhāsabhedasmiṃ, vakko vaṅke tisu’ccate;

Vijjā vede ca sippe ca, tivijjādo ca buddhiyaṃ.

'Vakka' is neuter in the sense of 'kidney'; the word 'vakka' is said to be of three genders when meaning 'crooked'. 'Vijjā' is used for the Vedas, for crafts, for the three knowledges and so forth, and for wisdom.

1035.

1035.

Samādhimhi pume’kaggo, nākule vāccaliṅgiko;

Pajjaṃ siloke, pajjo’ddhe, pajjo pādahite tisu.

'Ekagga' is masculine in the sense of 'concentration'; the word 'nakula' (mongoose) is of variable gender. 'Pajja' is neuter for a 'stanza'; it is masculine for 'half a verse'; and it is of three genders when meaning 'beneficial to the feet'.

1036.

1036.

Katako rukkhabhedasmiṃ, katako kittime tisu;

Vidheyye assavo tīsu, pubbamhī purise siyā.

'Kataka' is masculine for a 'species of tree'; the word 'kataka' is of three genders when meaning 'artificial'. 'Assava' is of three genders when meaning 'submissive'; the word 'pubba' (pus) may be masculine.

1037.

1037.

Kalyāṇe kathitaṃ khemaṃ, tīsu laddhattharakkhaṇe;

Atho niyojane vuttaṃ, kāriyepi payojanaṃ.

'Khema' is neuter when spoken of in the sense of 'goodness'; it is of three genders when meaning 'protecting what has been obtained'. Furthermore, 'payojana' is said in the sense of 'engagement' and also 'action'.

1038.

1038.

Assattho tīsu assāsa, ppatte, bodhiddume pume;

Tīsu luddo kurūre ca, nesādamhi pume siyā.

'Assattha' is of three genders when meaning 'having found comfort'; it is masculine when meaning 'the Bodhi tree'. 'Ludda' is of three genders when meaning 'cruel'; it may be masculine when meaning 'a hunter'.

1039.

1039.

Vilaggo tīsu laggasmiṃ, pume majjhamhi dissati;

Aḍḍho tvanitthiyaṃ bhāge, dhanimhi vāccaliṅgiko.

'Vilagga' is of three genders when meaning 'attached'; it is seen as masculine when meaning 'the middle'. 'Aḍḍha' is not feminine when meaning 'a part'; it is of variable gender when meaning 'rich'.

1040.

1040.

Kaṭṭhaṃ dārumhi, taṃ kicche, gahane kasite tisu;

Sasantāne ca visaye, gocare’ jjhatta muccateti.

'Kaṭṭha' is neuter for 'wood'; that same word is of three genders when meaning 'difficult', 'thicket', or 'ploughed'. 'Ajjhatta' is said to be used in the sense of 'one's own continuity', 'domain', and 'pasture'.

Iti addhānekatthavaggo.

Thus the chapter on words with various meanings.

1041.

1041.

Bhuvane ca jane loko, moretvaggimhi so sikhī;

Siloko tu yase pajje,

Rukkhe tu sāmike dhavo.

'Loka' is used for 'world' and 'people'; 'sikhī' for 'peacock' and 'fire'; 'siloka' for 'fame' and 'verse'; 'dhavo' for 'tree' and 'master'.

1042.

1042.

Vaṭabyāmesu nigrodho, dhaṅko tu vāyase bake;

Vāro tva’vasarā’hesu, kucetvabbhe payodharo.

'Nigrodha' is used for 'banyan tree' and 'fathom'; 'dhaṅka' for 'crow' and 'heron'; 'vāra' for 'opportunity' and 'day'; 'payodhara' for 'breast' and 'cloud'.

1043.

1043.

Ucchaṅge lakkhaṇe cā’ṅko, rasmi’tthī juti rajjusu;

Diṭṭho’bhāsesu āloko,

Buddho tu paṇḍite jine.

'Aṅka' is used for 'lap' and 'mark'; 'rasmi' is feminine, used for 'radiance' and 'rope'. 'Āloka' is seen in the sense of 'splendour'; 'buddho' for 'a wise one' and 'a conqueror'.

1044.

1044.

Sūraṃ’sūsu pume bhānū, daṇḍo tu muggare dame;

Devamacchesva’nimiso, pattho tu mānasānusu.

'Bhānu' is masculine, used for 'sun' and 'ray'; 'daṇḍa' for 'club' and 'punishment'; 'animisa' for 'gods' and 'fish'; 'pattha' for 'a measure' and 'a mountain plateau'.

1045.

1045.

Ātaṅko roga tāpesu,

Mātaṅgo sapace gaje;

Migo pasu kuruṅgesu, ulūki’ndesu kosiyo.

'Ātaṅka' is used for 'disease' and 'affliction'; 'mātaṅga' for 'an outcaste' and 'an elephant'; 'miga' for 'beast' and 'antelope'; 'kosiyo' for 'owl' and 'Indra'.

1046.

1046.

Viggaho [M 0.0085] kalahe kāye, puriso māṇava’ttasu;

Dāyādo bandhave putte, sire sīsaṃ tipumhi ca.

'Viggaha' is used for 'quarrel' and 'body'; 'purisa' for 'man' and 'self'; 'dāyāda' for 'relative' and 'son'; and 'sīsa' for 'head' and 'lead', and it is of three genders.

1047.

1047.

Balihatthaṃ’sūsu karo, dante vippe’ṇḍaje dvijo;

Vattaṃ pajjā’nanā’cāre, dhaññaṅge sukhume kaṇo.

'Kara' is used for 'tax', 'hand', and 'ray'; 'dvija' for 'tooth', 'brahmin', and 'the egg-born'. 'Vatta' is used for 'verse', 'face', and 'conduct'; 'kaṇa' for 'a particle of grain' and 'a minute particle'.

1048.

1048.

Thambho thūṇa jaḷattesu, sūpo kummāsa byañjane;

Gaṇḍo phoṭe kapolamhi, aggho mūlye ca pūjane.

'Thambha' is used for 'pillar' and 'rigidity'; 'sūpa' for 'sour gruel' and 'curry'; 'gaṇḍa' for 'boil' and 'cheek'; 'aggha' for 'price' and 'honour'.

1049.

1049.

Pakāro tulya bhedesu, sakunto bhāsapakkhisu;

Bhāgye vidhi vidhāne ca, sare khagge ca sāyako.

'Pakāra' is used for 'similarity' and 'difference'; 'sakunta' for 'vulture' and 'bird'; 'vidhi' for 'fate' and 'rule'; 'sāyaka' for 'arrow' and 'sword'.

1050.

1050.

Sāraṅgo cātake eṇe, pattī tu sarapakkhisu;

Sede pāko vipāke tha,

Bhikkhubhede caye gaṇo.

'Sāraṅga' is used for the 'cātaka bird' and 'deer'; 'pattī' for 'foot-soldier' and 'bird'; 'pāka' for 'cooking' and 'result'; 'gaṇa' for 'a chapter of monks' and 'a heap'.

1051.

1051.

Rāsi puñje ca mesā’do,

Asse loṇe ca sindhavo;

Saṃvaṭṭe palayo nāse, pūgo kamukarāsisu.

'Rāsi' is used for 'heap' and 'the ram, etc.'; 'sindhava' for 'horse' and 'rock-salt'; 'palaya' for 'cosmic dissolution' and 'destruction'; 'pūga' for 'areca-nut tree' and 'multitude'.

1052.

1052.

Amate tu sudhā lepe, abhikhyā nāma raṃsisu;

Satti sāmatthiye satthe, mahī najjantare bhuvi;

Līlā kriyā vilāsesu,

Satte tu atraje pajā.

'Sudhā' is used for 'nectar' and 'plaster'; 'abhikhyā' for 'name' and 'radiance'; 'satti' for 'ability' and 'weapon'; 'mahī' for 'river-island' and 'the earth'; 'līlā' for 'action' and 'grace'; 'pajā' for 'beings' and 'offspring'.

1053.

1053.

Ñāṇe lābhe upaladdhi, paveṇī kuthaveṇisu;

Pavatti vutti vattāsu, vetane bharaṇe bhati.

'Upaladdhi' is used for 'knowledge' and 'gain'; 'paveṇī' for 'tradition' and 'a braid of hair'; 'pavatti' for 'occurrence' and 'news'; 'bhati' for 'wage' and 'sustenance'.

1054.

1054.

Ācārepi mariyādā, bhūti sattā samiddhisu;

Soppe pamāde tandī ca, yātrā gamana vuttisu.

'Mariyādā' is used for 'conduct' and 'boundary'; 'bhūti' for 'existence' and 'prosperity'; 'tandī' for 'sleep' and 'sloth'; 'yātrā' for 'journey' and 'livelihood'.

1055.

1055.

Nindā kucchā’pavādesu, kaṅgu dhañña piyaṅgusu;

Mokkhesive samesanti, vibhāge bhatti sevane.

'Nindā' is used for 'blame' and 'reproach'; 'kaṅgu' for 'millet' and 'the priyangu plant'; 'bhatti' for 'division' and 'devotion'.

1056.

1056.

Icchāyaṃ jutiyaṃ kanti, rañjane sūrate rati;

Gehe vasati vāse tha, nadī senāsu vāhinī.

'Kanti' is used for 'desire' and 'splendour'; 'rati' for 'attachment' and 'sexual love'; 'vasati' for 'house' and 'dwelling'; 'vāhinī' for 'river' and 'army'.

1057.

1057.

Patthe nāḷe ca nāḷi’tthī, gaṇe samiti saṅgame;

Taṇhā lobhe pipāsāyaṃ, magga vuttīsu vattanī.

'Nāḷi' is feminine, used for 'a measure' and 'a tube'; 'samiti' for 'group' and 'assembly'; 'taṇhā' for 'greed' and 'thirst'; 'vattanī' for 'path' and 'conduct'.

1058.

1058.

Pāṇyaṅge nābhi cakkante, yāce viññatti ñāpane;

Vitti tose vedanāyaṃ, ṭhāne tu jīvite ṭhiti.

'Nābhi' is used for 'navel' and 'the hub of a wheel'; 'viññatti' for 'request' and 'announcement'; 'vitti' for 'joy' and 'feeling'; 'ṭhiti' for 'state' and 'duration'.

1059.

1059.

Taraṅge [M 0.0086] cā’ntare vīci, dhīratte dhāraṇe dhiti;

Bhū bhūmiyañca bhamuke, sadde vede save suti.

'Vīci' is used for 'wave' and 'interval'; 'dhiti' for 'courage' and 'retention'; 'bhū' for 'the earth' and 'the eyebrow'; 'suti' for 'sound', 'sacred text', and 'hearing'.

1060.

1060.

Gottaṃ nāme ca vaṃse tha, nagare ca ghare puraṃ;

Okaṃ tu nissaye gehe, kulaṃ tu gottarāsisu.

'Gotta' is used for 'name' and 'lineage'; 'pura' for 'city' and 'house'; 'oka' for 'refuge' and 'house'; 'kula' for 'lineage' and 'multitude'.

1061.

1061.

Heme vitte hiraññañca, paññāṇaṃ tva’ṅka vuddhisu;

Athā’mbarañca khe vatthe, guyhaṃ liṅge rahasya’pi.

'Hirañña' is used for 'gold' and 'wealth'; 'paññāṇa' for 'mark' and 'growth'; 'ambara' for 'sky' and 'garment'; 'guyha' for 'private part' and 'a secret'.

1062.

1062.

Tapo dhamme vate ceva, pāpe tvā’gumhi kibbisaṃ;

Ratanaṃ maṇi seṭṭhesu, vassaṃ hāyana vuṭṭhisu.

'Tapo' is used for 'Dhamma' and 'vow'; 'kibbisa' for 'evil' and 'wrongdoing'; 'ratana' for 'gem' and 'the best'; 'vassa' for 'year' and 'rain'.

1063.

1063.

Vanaṃ arañña vārīsu, khīramhi tu jale payo;

Akkharaṃ lipi mokkhesu, methūnaṃ saṅgame rate.

'Vana' is used for 'forest' and 'water'; 'payo' for 'milk' and 'water'; 'akkhara' for 'script' and 'liberation'; 'methūna' for 'union' and 'sexual intercourse'.

1064.

1064.

Sotaṃ kaṇṇe payovege, riṭṭhaṃ pāpā’subhesu ca;

Āgu pāpā’parādhesu, ketumhi cihane dhajo.

'Sota' is used for 'ear' and 'water current'; 'riṭṭha' for 'evil' and 'inauspicious'; 'āgu' for 'sin' and 'offence'; 'dhaja' for 'banner' and 'mark'.

1065.

1065.

Gopuraṃ dvāramattepi, mandiraṃ nagare ghare;

Vāccaliṅgā paramito, byatto tu paṇḍite phuṭe.

'Gopura' is used for 'gateway' and 'door'; 'mandira' for 'city' and 'house'. From here onwards, the words are of variable gender. 'Byatta' is used for 'learned' and 'clear'.

1066.

1066.

Vallabho dayite’jjhakkhe, jale thūlo mahatyapi;

Kurūre bherave bhīmo,

Lolo tu lolupe cale.

'Vallabha' is used for 'beloved' and 'overseer'; 'thūla' for 'dull' and 'large'; 'bhīma' for 'cruel' and 'frightful'; 'lola' for 'greedy' and 'unsteady'.

1067.

1067.

Bībhaccho vikate bhīme, komale tikhiṇe mudu;

Iṭṭhe ca madhure sādu, sādumhi madhuro piye.

'Bībhaccha' is used for 'repulsive' and 'frightful'; 'mudu' for 'soft' and 'blunt'; 'sādu' for 'pleasant' and 'sweet'; 'madhura' for 'sweet' and 'dear'.

1068.

1068.

Site tu suddhe odāto, dvijivho sūcakā’hisu;

Sakke samattho sambandhe, samattaṃ niṭṭhitā’khile.

'Odāta' is used for 'white' and 'pure'; 'dvijivha' for 'tale-bearer' and 'serpent'; 'sakka' for 'capable' and 'related'; 'samatta' for 'finished' and 'entire'.

1069.

1069.

Suddho kevala pūtesu, jighañño’ntā’dhamesu ca;

Poṇo’panata ninnesu, añña nīcesu ce’taro.

'Suddha' is used for 'mere' and 'pure'; 'jighañña' for 'last' and 'base'; 'poṇa' for 'bent down' and 'sloping'; 'itara' for 'other' and 'low'.

1070.

1070.

Suci suddhe site pūte, pesalo dakkhacārusu;

Adhamo kucchite ūne, appiye pya’liko bhave.

'Suci' is used for 'pure', 'white', and 'stainless'; 'pesala' for 'skilled' and 'charming'; 'adhama' for 'reproachable', 'deficient', and 'unlikable'; 'alika' for 'unlikable' and 'false'.

1071.

1071.

Byāpe asuddhe saṃkiṇṇo, bhabbaṃ yogye ca bhāvini;

Sukhumo appakā’ṇūsu, vuddho there ca paṇḍite.

'Saṃkiṇṇa' is used for 'pervaded' and 'impure'; 'bhabba' for 'capable' and 'future'; 'sukhuma' for 'small' and 'subtle'; 'vuddha' for 'elder' and 'wise'.

1072.

1072.

Subhe sādhumhi bhaddo tha, tyā’do ca vipule bahu;

Dhīro budhe dhitimante, vellitaṃ kuṭile dhute.

'Bhadda' is used for 'auspicious' and 'virtuous'; 'bahu' for 'extensive' and 'much'; 'dhīra' for 'wise' and 'resolute'; 'vellita' for 'crooked' and 'shaken'.

1073.

1073.

Visado [M 0.0087] byatta setesu, taruṇo tu yuve nave;

Yoggaṃ yāne, khame yoggo,

Piṇḍitaṃ gaṇite ghane.

'Visada' is used for 'clear' and 'white'; 'taruṇa' for 'youthful' and 'new'; 'yogga' for 'vehicle' and 'capable'; 'piṇḍita' for 'calculated' and 'dense'.

1074.

1074.

Budhe’bhijāto kulaje, vuddho’rūsu mahallako;

Kalyāṇaṃ sundare cāpi, himo tu sītalepi ca.

'Abhijāta' is used for 'wise' and 'of noble birth'; 'mahallaka' for 'old' and 'large'; 'kalyāṇa' for 'beautiful' and 'virtuous'; 'hima' for 'frost' and 'cold'.

1075.

1075.

Lole tu sīghe capalo, vutte udita muggate;

Āditte gabbite ditto, piṭṭhaṃ tu cuṇṇitepi ca.

'Capala' is used for 'restless' and 'swift'; 'udita' for 'spoken' and 'arisen'; 'ditta' for 'blazing' and 'proud'; 'piṭṭha' for 'ground' or 'powdered'.

1076.

1076.

Vigate vāyite vāyane (ka.) vītaṃ, bhāvitaṃ vaḍḍhitepi ca;

Bhajjite patite bhaṭṭho, puṭṭho pucchita posite.

'Vīta' is used for 'gone away' and 'woven'; 'bhāvita' for 'developed' and 'cultivated'; 'bhaṭṭha' for 'roasted' and 'fallen'; 'puṭṭha' for 'asked' and 'nourished'.

1077.

1077.

Jāto bhūte caye jātaṃ,

Paṭibhāgo samā’risu;

Sūro vīre ravisūre, duṭṭho kuddhe ca dūsite.

'Jāta' is used for 'born' and 'heap'; 'paṭibhāga' for 'equal' and 'enemy'; 'sūra' for 'hero' and 'sun'; 'duṭṭha' for 'angry' and 'corrupted'.

1078.

1078.

Diṭṭho’rimhi’kkhite diṭṭho diṭṭhaṃ (ka.),

Mūḷhe pote ca bāliso;

Nindāyaṃ khepane khepo, niyamo nicchaye vate.

'Diṭṭha' is used for 'enemy' and 'seen'; 'bālisa' for 'foolish' and 'a young animal'; 'khepa' for 'blame' and 'throwing'; 'niyama' for 'resolution' and 'vow'.

1079.

1079.

Salākāyaṃ kuso dabbhe,

Bālyādo tu khaye vayo;

Lepa gabbesva’valepo, aṇḍajo mīnapakkhisu.

'Kusa' is used for 'a grass stalk' and 'dabbha grass'; 'vaya' for 'youth' and 'decay'; 'avalepa' for 'plaster' and 'pride'; 'aṇḍaja' for 'fish' and 'bird'.

1080.

1080.

Bilāle nakule babbu, mantho manthanasattusu;

Vālo kese’ssādilome,

Saṅghāto ghātarāsisu.

'Babbu' is used for 'cat' and 'mongoose'; 'mantha' for 'churning stick' and 'barley-meal'; 'vāla' for 'hair' and 'tail-hair'; 'saṅghāta' for 'killing' and 'heap'.

1081.

1081.

Gopagāme rave ghoso, sūto sārathivandisu;

Mālyaṃ tu pupphe taddāme, vāho tu sakaṭe haye.

'Ghosa' is used for 'cowherd village' and 'sound'; 'sūta' for 'charioteer' and 'bard'; 'mālya' for 'flower' and 'garland'; 'vāha' for 'cart' and 'horse'.

1082.

1082.

Khaye’ccane cā’pacayo, kālo samaya maccusu;

Bhe tārakā nettamajjhe, sīmā’ vadhi, ṭṭhitīsu ca.

'Apacaya' is used for 'decay' and 'worship'; 'kāla' for 'time' and 'death'; 'bha' for 'star' and 'pupil of the eye'; 'sīmā' for 'boundary' and 'fixed position'.

1083.

1083.

Ābhogo puṇṇatā’vajje-

Svā’ḷi’tthī sakhi setusu;

Satte thūle tīsu daḷho, latā sākhāya valliyaṃ.

'Ābhoga' is used for 'fullness' and 'reflection'; 'āḷi' is feminine and used for 'female friend' and 'dam'; 'daḷha', which is of three genders, is used for 'attached' and 'solid'; 'latā' is used for 'branch' and 'creeper'.

1084.

1084.

Mutti’tthī [M 0.0088] mocane mokkhe,

Kāyo tu deha rāsisu;

Nīce puthujjano mūḷhe, bhattā sāmini dhārake.

'Mutti' is feminine and is used for 'release' and 'liberation'; 'kāya' for 'body' and 'aggregate'; 'puthujjana' for 'low' and 'deluded'; 'bhattā' for 'master' and 'supporter'.

1085.

1085.

Sikhā, piñchesu sikhaṇḍo, satte tva’ttani puggalo;

Sambādho saṃkaṭe guyhe,

Nāse khepe parābhavo.

'Sikhaṇḍa' is used for 'crest' and 'peacock's tail'; 'puggala' for 'being' and 'self'; 'sambādha' for 'confinement' and 'secret'; 'parābhava' for 'destruction' and 'defeat'.

1086.

1086.

Vacco rūpe karīse tha, juti’tthī kantiraṃsisu;

'Vacco' is used for 'form' and 'excrement'; 'juti' is feminine and is used for 'beauty' and 'radiance'.

1087.

1087.

Labbhaṃ yutte ca laddhabbe, khaṇḍe paṇṇe dalaṃ mataṃ;

Sallaṃ kaṇḍe salākāyaṃ, sucitte dhāvanaṃ gate;

Bhantatthe vibbhamo hāve, moho’vijjāya mucchane.

'Labbha' is used for 'suitable' and 'obtainable'; 'dala' is considered for 'fragment' and 'leaf'; 'salla' for 'dart' and 'splinter'; 'dhāvana' for 'adornment' and 'running'; 'vibbhamo' for 'agitation' and 'charm'; 'moha' for 'ignorance' and 'fainting'.

1088.

1088.

Sedo ghammajale pāke,

Goḷe ucchumaye guḷo;

Mitte sahāye ca sakhā, vibhū niccappabhūsu so.

'Seda' is used for 'sweat' and 'cooking'; 'guḷa' for 'ball' and 'molasses'; 'sakhā' for 'friend' and 'companion'; 'vibhū' for 'eternal' and 'lord'.

1089.

1089.

Khagge kurūre nettiṃso, parasmiṃ atra tīsva’mu;

Kalaṅko’ṅkā’pavādesu,

Dese janapado jane.

'Nettiṃsa' is used for 'sword' and 'cruel'; 'amu', in three genders, is used for 'that' and 'this'; 'kalaṅka' for 'stain' and 'blame'; 'janapada' for 'country' and 'people'.

1090.

1090.

Pajje gāthā vacībhede, vaṃso tva’nvayaveṇusu;

Yānaṃ rathādo gamane, sarūpasmiṃ adho talaṃ.

'Gāthā' is used for 'verse' and 'a type of speech'; 'vaṃsa' for 'lineage' and 'bamboo'; 'yāna' for 'vehicle' and 'going'; 'tala' for 'nature' and 'a lower part'.

1091.

1091.

Majjho vilagge vemajjhe, pupphaṃ tu kusumo’tusu;

Sīlaṃ sabhāve subbate, puṅgavo usabhe vare.

'Majjha' is used for 'waist' and 'the middle'; 'puppha' for 'flower' and 'menses'; 'sīla' for 'nature' and 'good observance'; 'puṅgava' for 'bull' and 'excellent'.

1092.

1092.

Kose khagādibīje’ṇḍaṃ, kuharaṃ gabbhare bile;

Nettiṃse gaṇḍake khaggo, kadambo tu dume caye.

Aṇḍa is used for a testicle and an egg; kuhara for a cave and a hole; khagga for a sword and a rhinoceros; kadamba for a tree and a multitude.

1093.

1093.

Bhe’dhenuyaṃ rohiṇī’tthī, varaṅgaṃ yoniyaṃ sire;

Akkose sapathe sāpo, paṅkaṃ pāpe ca kaddame.

Rohiṇī is used for a star, a cow, and a woman; varaṅga for the female organ and the head; sāpa for a curse and an oath; paṅka for evil and mire.

1094.

1094.

Bhogavatyu’rage bhogī, ssaro tu sivasāmisu;

Bale pabhāve vīriyaṃ, tejo tesu ca dittiyaṃ.

Bhogī is used for a female serpent and a serpent; issara for Śiva and a master; vīriya for strength and power; teja for these and for radiance.

1095.

1095.

Dhārā santati khaggaṅge, vānaṃ taṇhāya sibbane;

Khattā sūte paṭihāre, vitti pīḷāsu vedanā.

Dhārā is used for continuity and a sword's edge; vāna for craving and sewing; khattā for a charioteer and a doorkeeper; vedanā for experience and suffering.

1096.

1096.

Thiyaṃ [M 0.0089] mati’cchāpaññāsu, pāpe yuddhe rave raṇo;

Lavotu bindu’cchedesu, palāle’ palāse (ṭī.), palāpe-ṭī (453-1124) gāthā passitabbā tisaye bhusaṃ.

Mati is used for a woman, desire, and wisdom; raṇa for evil, battle, and sound; lava for a drop and cutting; bhusa for straw and excess.

1097.

1097.

Bādhā dukkhe nisedhe ca, mūlapadepi mātikā;

Sneho tele’dhikappeme,

Gharā’pekkhāsu ālayo.

Bādhā is used for suffering and restraint; mātikā for a source text; sneha for oil and excessive love; ālaya for a house and craving.

1098.

1098.

Ketusmiṃ ketanaṃ gehe, ṭhāne bhūmi’tthiyaṃ bhuvi;

Lekhye lekho rāji lekhā, pūjite bhagavā jine.

Ketana is used for a banner and a house; bhūmi for a place, a woman, and the earth; lekha for writing, and lekhā for a line; bhagavā for one who is honored and a conqueror.

1099.

1099.

Gadā satthe gado roge, nisajjā pīṭhesvā’sanaṃ;

Tathāgato jine satte,

Caye dehe samussayo.

Gadā is used for a weapon, and gada for disease; āsana for sitting and a seat; Tathāgata for a conqueror and a being; samussaya for an aggregate and the body.

1100.

1100.

Bilaṃ koṭṭhāsa chiddesu, vajjaṃ dose ca bheriyaṃ;

Kāle dīghañjase’ddhānaṃ, āliyaṃ setu kāraṇe.

Bila is used for a portion and a hole; vajja for a fault and a drum; addhāna for time, a long distance, and a path; āliya for a bridge and a reason.

1101.

1101.

Okāso kāraṇe dese,

Sabhā gehe ca saṃsade;

Yūpo thambhe ca pāsāde, ayanaṃ gamane pathe.

Okāsa is used for a reason and a place; sabhā for a house and an assembly; yūpa for a pillar and a palace; ayana for going and a path.

1102.

1102.

Akko rukkhantare sūre,

Asso koṇe hayepi ca;

Aṃso khandhe ca koṭṭhāse,

Jālaṃ sūsva’cci no pume.

Akka is used for a specific tree and the sun; assa for a corner and a horse; aṃsa for the shoulder and a part; jāla for a net and a flame, and it is not masculine.

1103.

1103.

Nāsā’sattesva’ bhāvo tha,

Anna modana bhuttisu;

Jīvaṃ pāne jane jīvo,

Ghāso tva’nne ca bhakkhaṇe.

Abhāva is used for destruction and non-existence; anna for delight and a meal; jīva for life-breath and a person; ghāsa for food and eating.

1104.

1104.

Chadane’cchādanaṃ vatthe, nikāyo geharāsisu;

Annādo āmisaṃ maṃse; Dikkhā tu yajane’ccane.

Acchādana is used for a covering and cloth; nikāya for a house and a collection; āmisa for food and flesh; dikkhā for sacrifice and worship.

1105.

1105.

Kriyāyaṃ kārikā pajje,

Ketu tu cihane dhaje;

Kusumaṃ thīraje pupphe, vānare tu budhe kavi.

Kārikā is used for an action and a verse; ketu for an emblem and a flag; kusuma for menses and a flower; kavi for a monkey and a wise person.

1106.

1106.

Adhare [M 0.0090] kharabhe oṭṭho, luddo tu luddakepi ca;

Kalusaṃ tvā’vile pāpe, pāpe kali parājaye.

Oṭṭha is used for the lower lip and a young camel; ludda for a hunter; kalusa for the turbid and evil; kali for evil and defeat.

1107.

1107.

Kantāro vana, duggesu, caro cāramhi cañcale;

Janāvāse gaṇe gāmo, cammaṃ tu phalake tace.

Kantāra is used for a forest and a difficult place; cara for a spy and the fickle; gāma for a human dwelling and a group; camma for a shield and skin.

1108.

1108.

Āmodo hāsa gandhesu,

Cāru tu kanakepi ca;

Sattāyaṃ bhavanaṃ gehe, lese tu khalite chalaṃ.

Āmoda is used for joy and fragrance; cāru for gold; bhavana for existence and a house; chala for a pretext and a mistake.

1109.

1109.

Veraṃ pāpe ca paṭighe, taco cammani vakkale;

Ucce’dhirohe āroho, nettaṃ vatthantara’kkhisu.

Vera is used for evil and hostility; taca for hide and bark; āroha for high and climbing; netta for a type of cloth and the eye.

1110.

1110.

Paṭihāre mukhe dvāraṃ, pete ñāte mato tisu;

Māso paraṇṇa kālesu, naggo tva’celakepi ca.

Dvāra is used for a doorkeeper and an entrance; mata is used for the deceased, the known, and an opinion; māsa for a bean and a month; nagga for a naked ascetic.

1111.

1111.

Dose ghāte ca paṭigho, migādo chagale pasu;

Arūpe cā’vhaye nāmaṃ, daro daratha bhītisu.

Paṭigha is used for anger and slaying; pasu for a beast of prey and a goat; nāma for the formless and a name; dara for anxiety and fear.

1112.

1112.

Yācane bhojane bhikkhā,

Bhāre tva’tisaye bharo;

Dabbi’ndajāyāsu sujā, meghe tva’bbhaṃ vihāyase.

Bhikkhā is used for begging and food; bhara for a burden and excess; Sujā for a ladle and Indra's wife; abbha for a cloud and the sky.

1113.

1113.

Modako khajjabhedepi, maṇike ratane maṇi;

Selā’rāmesu malayo,

Sabhāva’ṅkesu lakkhaṇaṃ.

Modaka is used for a type of confection; maṇi for a water pot and a gem; malaya for a mountain and a park; lakkhaṇa for nature and a mark.

1114.

1114.

Havi sappimhi hotabbe, siro seṭṭhe ca muddhani;

Vicārepi viveko tha, sikharī pabbate dume.

Havi is used for ghee and what should be offered; sira for the best and the head; viveka for investigation; sikharī for a mountain and a tree.

1115.

1115.

Vego jave pavāhe ca, saṅku tu khilahetisu;

Niggahīte kaṇe bindu, varāho sūkare gaje.

Vega is used for speed and a flow; saṅku for a stake and a cause; bindu for the anusvāra and a drop; varāha for a swine and an elephant.

1116.

1116.

Nettante cittake’pāṅgaṃ, siddhattho sāsape jine;

Hāro muttāguṇe gāhe,

Khārako makuḷe rase.

Apāṅga is used for the corner of the eye and a sectarian mark; siddhattha for a mustard seed and a conqueror; hāra for a pearl-string and seizing; khāraka for a bud and a flavor.

1117.

1117.

Accayo’ tikkame dose,

Selarukkhesva’go nago;

Svappe’vadhāraṇe mattaṃ, apacitya’ccane khaye.

Accaya is used for transgression and a fault; aga and naga for a mountain and a tree; matta for a small amount and emphasis; apaciti for reverence and decay.

1118.

1118.

Chiddo’taraṇesvo’tāro,

Brahme [M 0.0091] ca janake pitā;

Pitāmaho’yyake brahme,

Poto nāvāya bālake.

Otāra is used for a fault and a crossing; pitā for Brahmā and a progenitor; pitāmaha for a grandfather and Brahmā; pota for a boat and a child.

1119.

1119.

Rukkhe vaṇṇe sune soṇo,

Sagge tu gagane divo;

Vatthe gandhe ghare vāso, cullo khudde ca uddhane.

Soṇa is used for a tree, the color red, and a dog; diva for heaven and the sky; vāsa for cloth, scent, and a house; culla for the small and a hearth.

1120.

1120.

Kaṇṇo koṇe ca savaṇe,

Mālā pupphe ca pantiyaṃ;

Bhāgo bhāgye’kadesesu,

Kuṭṭhaṃ roge’ japālake.

Kaṇṇa is used for a corner and the ear; mālā for a garland and a row; bhāga for fortune and a portion; kuṭṭha for a disease and a goatherd.

1121.

1121.

Seyyā senāsane sene, cundabhaṇḍamhi ca’bbhamo;

Vatthādilomaṃ’su kare, nipāto patane’byaye.

Seyyā is used for a resting place and for lying down; abbhama for a turner's lathe; aṃsu for fiber and a ray; nipāta for falling and an indeclinable particle.

1122.

1122.

Sākhāyaṃ viṭapo thambhe, sattu khajjantare dise;

Sāmiko pati’yiresu, paṭṭhānaṃ gati hetusu.

Viṭapa (branch) means 'branch' and 'pillar'; sattu (enemy/groats) means 'eatable' and 'enemy'; sāmika (owner/husband) means 'husband' and 'master'; paṭṭhāna (foundation/origin) means 'going' and 'cause'.

1123.

1123.

Rāge raṅgo naccaṭṭhāne, pānaṃ peyye ca pītiyaṃ;

Iṇu’kkhepesu uddhāro, ummāre eḷako aje.

Rāga (passion/color) means 'color' and 'dancing place'; pāna (drink/drinking) means 'drinkable' and 'joy'; uddhāra (raising/removal) means 'debt' and 'raising'; eḷaka (ram/sheep) means 'threshold' and 'goat'.

1124.

1124.

Pahāro pothane yāme,

Sarado hāyano’tusu;

Kuṇḍikāyā’ḷhake tumbo,

Palālo palāpo (ka.) 453-gāthā passitabbā tu bhusamhi ca.

Pahāra (blow/strike) means 'striking' and 'a watch of the night'; sarada (autumn) means 'year' and 'season'; tumba (gourd) means 'water pot' and 'āḷhaka measure'; palāla (straw) also means 'chaff'.

1125.

1125.

Matā’vāṭe caye kāsu, panisā kāraṇe raho;

Kāso poṭagale roge,

Doso kodhe guṇe’tare.

Kāsu (pit) means 'charnel pit' and 'heap'; panisā means 'reason' and 'solitude'; kāsa (cough) means 'a type of grass' and 'disease'; dosa (fault/anger) means 'anger' and 'defect'.

1126.

1126.

Yutya’ṭṭāla’ṭṭitesva’ṭṭo, kīḷāyaṃ kānane davo;

Uppattiyaṃ co’ppatanaṃ, uyyānaṃ gamane vane.

Aṭṭa (lawsuit/tower/distress) means 'legal proceeding', 'watchtower', and 'afflicted'; dava (play/forest) means 'play' and 'forest'; uppatana (flying up) also means 'origination'; uyyāna (park) means 'going' and 'forest'.

1127.

1127.

Vokāro lāmake khandhe, mūlo’padāsu pābhataṃ;

Dasā’ vatthā paṭantesu, kāraṇaṃ ghāta, hetusu.

Vokāra (component part) means 'inferior' and 'aggregate'; mūla (root) means 'misfortune' and 'gift'; dasā (condition/hem) means 'state' and 'edge of a garment'; kāraṇa (cause) means 'killing' and 'cause'.

1128.

1128.

Hatthidāne [M 0.0092] mado gabbe, ghaṭā ghaṭana rāsisu;

Upahāro’bhihārepi, cayo bandhana rāsisu.

Mada (intoxication/pride) means 'rut-fluid of an elephant' and 'pride'; ghaṭā (cluster) means 'striving' and 'mass'; upahāra (offering) also means 'bringing near'; caya (heap) means 'binding' and 'mass'.

1129.

1129.

Gandho thoke ghāyanīye,

Cāgo tu dānahānisu;

Pāne pamode pīti’tthī, iṇe givā galepi ca.

Gandha (scent) means 'a little' and 'that which is to be smelled'; cāga (abandonment) means 'giving' and 'loss'; pīti (joy) means 'drink', 'delight', and 'woman'; gīvā (neck) means 'debt' and 'throat'.

1130.

1130.

Patiṭṭhā nissaye ṭhāne, balakkārepi sāhasaṃ;

Bhaṅgo bhede paṭe bhaṅgaṃ, chattaṃ tu chavakepi ca.

Patiṭṭhā (foundation) means 'support' and 'place'; sāhasa (violence) also means 'force'; bhaṅga (breaking/hemp) means 'splitting' and 'hempen cloth'; chatta (umbrella) also means 'mushroom'.

1131.

1131.

Ñāṇe bhuvi ca bhūri’ttī, anaṅge madano dume;

Pamātaripi mātā tha, veṭhu’ṇīsesu veṭhanaṃ.

Bhūri (abundance) means 'wisdom', 'earth', and 'woman'; madana (god of love) means 'the bodiless one' and 'tree'; mātā (mother) also means 'a standard of measure'; veṭhana (wrapping) means 'bandage' and 'turban'.

1132.

1132.

Māriso taṇḍuleyye’yye,

Mokkho nibbāna muttisu;

Indo’dhipati sakkesvā, rammaṇaṃ hetu gocare.

Mārisa (sir) means 'a kind of spinach' and 'noble one'; mokkha (release) means 'Nibbāna' and 'liberation'; inda (lord) means 'sovereign' and 'Sakka'; ārammaṇa (object) means 'cause' and 'sense-field'.

1133.

1133.

Aṅke saṇṭhāna mākāre,

Khette vappe (pākāramūle nettajale usume ca vappo-ṭī) vappo taṭepi ca;

Sammutya’nuññā vohāre, sva’tha lājāsu cā’kkhataṃ.

Aṅka (mark) means 'shape' and 'form'; vappa (sowing) means 'field' and 'bank'; sammuti (convention) means 'permission' and 'common usage'; akkhata (unbroken) also means 'parched grain'.

1134.

1134.

Satraṃ yāge sadādāne,

Somo tu osadhi’ndusu;

Saṅghāto yugagehaṅge, khāro ūse ca bhasmani.

Satra (sacrifice) means 'sacrifice' and 'constant giving'; soma (Soma plant/moon) means 'medicinal plant' and 'the moon'; saṅghāta (conjunction) means 'yoke' and 'part of a house'; khāra (alkaline substance) means 'saline soil' and 'ash'.

1135.

1135.

Ātāpo vīriye tāpe,

Bhāge sīmāya odhi cāti.

Ātāpa (heat/ardor) means 'energy' and 'heat'; odhi (limit) means 'part' and 'boundary'.

Iti pādānekatthavaggo.

Thus the Chapter on Words with Many Meanings.

Anekatthavaggo niṭṭhito.

The Chapter on Many Meanings is concluded.

3. Sāmaññakaṇḍa

3. The General Section

4. Abyayavagga

4. The Chapter on Indeclinables

1136.

1136.

Abyayaṃ vuccate dāni, cirassaṃ tu ciraṃ tathā;

Cirena cirarattāya, saha saddhiṃ samaṃ amā.

Now the indeclinables are spoken of. `Cirassaṃ`, `ciraṃ`, `cirena`, and `cirarattāya` mean 'for a long time'. `Saha`, `saddhiṃ`, `samaṃ`, and `amā` mean 'with' or 'together'.

1137.

1137.

Punappunaṃ [M 0.0093] abhiṇhañcā, sakiṃ cā’bhikkhaṇaṃ muhuṃ;

Vajjane tu vinā nānā, antarena rite puthu.

The words `punappunaṃ`, `abhiṇhaṃ`, `abhikkhaṇaṃ`, and `muhuṃ` mean 'repeatedly' or 'frequently'; `sakiṃ` means 'once'. In the sense of exclusion: `vinā`, `nānā`, `antarena`, `rite`, and `puthu`.

1138.

1138.

Balavaṃ suṭṭhu cā’tīvā, tisaye kimuta sva’ti;

Aho tu kiṃ kimū’ dāhu, vikappe kimuto’da ca.

In the sense of 'exceedingly': `balavaṃ`, `suṭṭhu`, `atīva`, `kimuta`, and `su ati`. In the sense of interrogation: `aho`, `kiṃ`, `kimu`, and `udāhu`. In the sense of 'alternative': `kimuto` and `uda`.

1139.

1139.

Avhāne bho are ambho,

Hambho re je’ṅga āvuso;

He hare tha kathaṃ kiṃsu, nanu kacci nu kiṃ samā.

In the sense of addressing: `bho`, `are`, `ambho`, `hambho`, `re`, `je`, `aṅga`, `āvuso`, `he`, and `hare`. In the sense of interrogation: `kathaṃ`, `kiṃsu`, `nanu`, `kacci`, `nu`, and `kiṃ`. Also the particles `tha` and `samā`.

1140.

1140.

Adhune’tarahī’dāni, sampati aññadatthu tu;

Tagghe’ kaṃse sasakkañcā, ddhā kāmaṃ jātu ve have.

The words `adhunā`, `etarahi`, `idāni`, and `sampati` mean 'now'. The word `aññadatthu` means 'on the other hand', and `tu` means 'but' or 'indeed'. In the sense of affirmation: `tagghe`, `ekaṃse`, `sasakkaṃ`, `addhā`, `kāmaṃ`, `jātu`, `ve`, and `have`.

1141.

1141.

Yāvatā tāvatā yāva, tāva kittāvatā tathā;

Ettāvatā ca kīve’ti, paricchedatthavācakā.

`Yāvatā`, `tāvatā`, `yāva`, `tāva`, `kittāvatā`, `tathā`, `ettāvatā`, and `kīva` are words that express the meaning of limitation.

1142.

1142.

Yathā tathā yatheve’vaṃ, yathānāma yathāhi ca;

Seyyathāpye’vamevaṃ, vā, tatheva ca yathāpi ca.

The following express comparison or manner: `yathā`, `tathā`, `yatheva`, `evaṃ`, `yathānāma`, `yathāhi`, `seyyathāpi`, `evamevaṃ`, `vā`, `tatheva`, and `yathāpi`.

1143.

1143.

Evampi ca seyyathāpi, nāma yatharivā’pi ca;

Paṭibhāgatthe yathāca, viya tatharivā’pi ca.

Also expressing comparison are: `evampi`, `seyyathāpi`, `nāma`, and `yathā iva api`. In the sense of 'likeness': `yathā`, `viya`, and `tathā iva api`.

1144.

1144.

Saṃ sāmañca sayaṃ cātha, āma sāhu lahū’pi ca;

Opāyikaṃ patirūpaṃ, sādhve’vaṃ sampaṭicchane.

`Saṃ` means 'together'; `sāmaṃ` and `sayaṃ` mean 'by oneself'; `āma` means 'yes'; `sāhu` means 'good'; `lahu` means 'quickly'; `opāyikaṃ` and `patirūpaṃ` mean 'suitable'. In the sense of acceptance: `sādhu` and `evaṃ`.

1145.

1145.

Yaṃ taṃ yato tato yena,

Tene’ti kāraṇe siyuṃ;

Asākalye tu cana ci, nipphale tu mudhā bhave.

`Yaṃ`, `taṃ`, `yato`, `tato`, `yena`, and `tena` are used in the sense of 'cause'. In the sense of indefiniteness: `cana` and `ci`. In the sense of 'fruitlessness': `mudhā`.

1146.

1146.

Kadāci jātu tulyā’tha, sabbato ca samantato;

Parito ca samantāpi, atha micchā musā bhave.

`Kadāci` and `jātu` mean 'at some time' or 'ever'; `tulyaṃ` means 'alike'; `atha` means 'then'. The words `sabbato`, `samantato`, `parito`, and `samantā` mean 'all around'. `Micchā` and `musā` mean 'falsely'.

1147.

1147.

Nisedhe na anomā’laṃ, nahi cetu sace yadi;

Anukulye tu saddhañca,

Nattaṃ rattaṃ (ṭī.) doso divā tva’he.

In the sense of negation: `na`, `no`, `mā`, `alaṃ`, and `nahi`. In the sense of condition: `ce`, `sace`, and `yadi`. In the sense of agreement: `saddhaṃ`. The words `nattaṃ` (by night) and `divā` (by day) are also indeclinables.

1148.

1148.

Īsaṃ kiñci manaṃ appe, sahasā tu atakkite;

Pure ggato tu purato, peccā’mutrabhavantare.

For 'a little': īsaṃ, kiñci, manaṃ, appe. For 'suddenly' and 'unexpectedly': sahasā, atakkite. For 'before' and 'ahead': pure, aggato, purato. For 'after death' and 'in another existence': peccā, amutra, bhavantare.

1149.

1149.

Aho [M 0.0094] hī vimhaye tuṇhī,

Tu mone thā’vi pātu ca;

Taṅkhaṇe sajju sapadi, balakkāre pasayha ca.

For wonder: aho, hī. For silence: tuṇhī, mone. For manifestation: thā, āvi, pātu. For 'at that moment': taṅkhaṇe, sajju, sapadi. For 'by force': balakkāre, pasayha.

1150.

1150.

Sudaṃ kho vo (ka.) assu yagghe ve,hā’dayo padapūraṇe;

Antarena’ntarā anto, vassaṃ nūna ca nicchaye.

'Sudaṃ', 'kho', 'assu', 'yagghe', 've', 'hā', and others are for filling the verse. For 'between' and 'within': antarena, antarā, anto. For 'certainty': vassaṃ, nūna.

1151.

1151.

Ānande sañca diṭṭhā tha, virodhakathane nanu;

Kāmappavedane kacci, usūyopagame’tthu ca.

For joy: saṃ, ca, diṭṭhā, tha. For speaking in contradiction: nanu. For expressing desire: kacci. For approaching with envy: atthu.

1152.

1152.

Evā’vadhāraṇe ñeyyaṃ, yathāttaṃ tu yathātathaṃ;

Nīcaṃ appe, mahatyu’ccaṃ, atha pāto page samā.

'Eva' is to be known for ascertainment. 'Yathāttaṃ' and 'yathātathaṃ' mean 'as it truly is'. 'Nīcaṃ' means 'low', 'appe' means 'a little', 'mahati' means 'greatly', and 'uccaṃ' means 'high'. 'Atha', 'pāto', and 'page' are similar.

1153.

1153.

Nicce sadā sanaṃ sanā (ka.) pāyo,

Bāhulye bāhiraṃ bahi;

Bahiddhā bāhirā bāhye, sīghetu saṇikaṃ bhave.

For 'always' or 'constantly': nicce, sadā, sanaṃ. For 'mostly': pāyo. For 'in abundance': bāhulye. For 'outside': bāhiraṃ, bahi, bahiddhā, bāhirā, bāhye. For 'quickly': sīghe. For 'gradually': saṇikaṃ.

1154.

1154.

Atthaṃ adassane duṭṭhu, nindāyaṃ, vandane namo;

Sammā suṭṭhu pasaṃsāyaṃ, atho sattāya matthi ca.

For 'disappearance': atthaṃ. For 'blame': duṭṭhu. For 'veneration': namo. For 'praise': sammā, suṭṭhu. And for 'existence': atthi.

1155.

1155.

Sāyaṃ sāye’jja atrā’he,

Suve tu sve anāgate;

Tato pare parasuve, hiyyotu divase gate.

For 'evening': sāyaṃ, sāye. For 'today' or 'on this day': ajja, atra, ahe. For 'tomorrow' or 'in the future': suve, sve, anāgate. For 'the day after tomorrow': tato pare, parasuve. For 'yesterday' or 'on a past day': hiyyo, divase gate.

1156.

1156.

Yattha yatra yahiṃcātha, tattha tatra tahiṃtahaṃ;

Atho uddhañca upari, heṭṭhā tu ca adho samā.

For 'where' or 'wherever': yattha, yatra, yahiṃ. For 'there' or 'in that place': tattha, tatra, tahiṃ, tahaṃ. The words 'uddhaṃ' and 'upari' mean 'upwards, above'; 'heṭṭhā' and 'adho' mean 'below, downwards', and are similar.

1157.

1157.

Codane iṅgha handā’tha, ārādūrā ca ārakā;

Parammukhā tu ca raho, sammukhā tvā’vi pātu ca.

For 'urging': iṅgha, handa, atha. For 'far' or 'distant': ārā, dūrā, ārakā. For 'in absence' or 'in secret': parammukhā, raho. For 'face to face' or 'openly': sammukhā, āvi, pātu.

1158.

1158.

Saṃsayatthamhi appeva, appevanāma nū’ti ca;

Nidassane iti’tthañca, evaṃ, kicche kathañci ca.

In the sense of doubt: appeva, appevanāma, nū. In illustration: iti, itthaṃ, evaṃ. In difficulty: kathañci.

1159.

1159.

Hā khede sacchi paccakkhe,

Dhuvaṃ thire’vadhāraṇe;

Tiro tu tiriyaṃ cātha, kucchāyaṃ duṭṭhu ku’ccate.

For sorrow: hā. For direct perception: sacchi, paccakkhe. For firmness and ascertainment: dhuvaṃ, thire. For 'across' or 'hidden': tiro, tiriyaṃ. For disgust, 'duṭṭhu' and 'ku' are said.

1160.

1160.

Suvatthi āsiṭṭhatthamhi, nindāyaṃ tu dhī dhi (ka.) kathyate;

Kuhiñcanaṃ kuhiṃ kutra, kuttha kattha kahaṃ kva tha.
